| **B-388** | HIGH | **FIXED 2026-05-28.** The source-gated DY-357 match smoke could stall during all-model `.pdmesh` extraction because promoted modeldefs can retain legacy small skeleton tokens such as `SKEL_HEAD` instead of real skeleton pointers. The skeleton metadata exporter treated every non-NULL `modeldef->skel` as dereferenceable, so head/model extraction could stop before the weapon match reached runtime source loading. Fix: skeleton symbol export now distinguishes small legacy skeleton IDs from pointers, explicitly preserves `SKEL_HEAD`, generated modeldef metadata lookup follows nested archive-member paths such as `.pdhead::mesh.pdmesh::_meta/manifest.json`, and the temporary one-model `0x019c` trace logs are removed. | `port/src/modasset_compiler.c`; `port/src/romextract_pdmesh.c`; `tests/test_asset_native_source_contract.cpp`; `tests/test_mod_external_archive_static.cpp` | Verified: focused `[modding][pdxxx][base][static][c3812]` PASS (383 assertions / 12 cases); focused `[modding][pdxxx][c3844][source][static]` PASS (193 assertions / 1 case); focused `[modding][pdxxx][c3844][weapon][static]` PASS (80 assertions / 1 case); `python tools\asset_native_source_guard.py` PASS; isolated `wpnskel` all-target build PASS; final `weapon_match_source_gate_smoke` PASS (35/35) with `base:dy357`, `base:model_dy357_hi`, and `base:model_cartridge_rifle` loaded from extracted public archive sources and no raw `Gdy357Z.bin`/`GcartridgeZ.bin` fallback. |

| **B-387** | HIGH | **FIXED 2026-05-28.** Strict archive conformance exposed that scenario `objects.tsv` `model_catalog_id` references named actual catalog IDs such as `base:model_chravenger`, but `.pdmesh` extraction only emitted weapon/head/body model archives. That left scenario model references catalog-valid but missing on disk, which breaks the clean archive closure contract. Fix: `.pdmesh` extraction now enumerates all `NUM_MODELS` `g_ModelStates` entries, uses `catalogModelIdByModelnum(modelnum)` for canonical readable IDs, bumps the fast-cache stamp, and emits readable `.pdmesh` archives for every catalog model reference while keeping numeric model slots private. | `port/src/romextract_pdmesh.c`; `tests/test_asset_native_source_contract.cpp` | Verified: focused `[modding][pdxxx][c3844][scenario][source_gate][static]` PASS (94 assertions / 1 case); isolated `c3844setupsrc` all-target build PASS; `weapon_match_source_gate_smoke` PASS (31/31); fresh smoke extraction emitted 728 `.pdmesh` archives including `base_model_chravenger.pdmesh`; strict generated-output conformance PASS with `--require-all-families` across 7,568 root / 8,569 checked archives; `python tools\asset_native_source_guard.py` PASS. |
| **B-386** | HIGH | **FIXED 2026-05-28.** The strict weapon match smoke exposed a source-pad corruption bug after `.pdscenario` pads/navigation were compiled from public source. `setupLoadFiles()` sorted `g_StageSetup.ailists` by scanning until `list == NULL`, but MP setup blobs can have a tiny/empty stage AI-list table; the unbounded sort then read and wrote past the setup blob into the next stage-pool allocation, which was the source-compiled pad buffer. Fix: stage AI-list conversion now computes a bounded table capacity from the loaded setup size, records the converted count, sorts only within that count, and sets `g_NumLvAilists` from the bounded count. Padfile promotion also keeps loaded-size checks for waypoint, waygroup, and cover tables so malformed source data fails closed instead of pointer-promoting garbage. | `src/game/setup.c`; `src/game/setuppads.c`; `src/include/game/setup.h`; `src/game/lv.c`; `tests/test_asset_native_source_contract.cpp`; `tests/test_catalog_provider_static.cpp` | Verified: `python tools\asset_native_source_guard.py` PASS; no temporary pad tracker references remain; focused `[modding][pdxxx][c3844][weapon][static],[modding][pdxxx][c3844][lang],[setup][combat-sim][static]` PASS (118 assertions / 3 cases); isolated `weaponrun` all-target build PASS; `weapon_archive_source_gate_smoke` PASS (14/14); `weapon_match_source_gate_smoke` PASS (31/31); `scenario_pads_source_gate_smoke` PASS (19/19). |

| **B-382** | HIGH | **FIXED 2026-05-28.** The source-only Debug gate treated any FileProvider handle as public source. That could let raw extraction/cache paths such as `data/<romid>/files/*.bin` or `data/<romid>/segments/*` pass the selected-family gate even though those are bootstrap/runtime-cache products, not clean typed-archive public source. Fix: `asset_source_debug` now rejects raw extracted ROM payload paths (`data/.../files`, `data/.../segments`, and `.bin`) when deciding whether a FileProvider handle satisfies source-only mode. This keeps c3844 from being falsely satisfied by switching RomProvider handles to raw extracted file handles. | `port/src/asset_source_debug.c`; `tests/test_asset_native_source_contract.cpp` | Verified: `python tools\asset_native_source_guard.py` PASS; focused `[modding][pdxxx][c3844][scenario][source_gate][static]` PASS (24 assertions / 1 case); session build `c3844rawgate` removed. |
| **B-381** | HIGH | **FIXED 2026-05-28.** Scenario source-only mode still let stage setup, briefing setup, pads, and tiles reach runtime through stage handles that can still be RomProvider-backed, and `setupLoadBriefing()` directly called `assetLoadRomToAddr(setupfilenum, ...)`. That masked Scenario archive chain gaps when `Debug.AssetSourceOnlyType=ASSET_SCENARIO`. | **B-380** | HIGH | **FIXED 2026-05-28.** The strict DY-357 weapon source smoke exposed that generated public-source body modeldefs were still treated like static/non-character modeldefs in player spawn paths. | **B-373** | MED | **FIXED 2026-05-26.** Mike reported that extracted asset folders showed many TSV files and did not look like the clean glTF/GLB archive contract. Audit found the real `.pdscenario` archives do contain `scene.glb` and decoded source tables, but the local output tree also contained `.zip` inspection copies and loose extracted folders under typed asset family directories, plus noisy duplicate `_meta/scenario/_meta/...sha256` sidecar trees when `.pdarena` embedded a `.pdscenario`. Root cause: c3812/c3841/c3842 had implemented the clean archive internals, but extraction/release guards did not clean or reject renamed `.zip` inspection copies, the arena closure copied nested scenario `_meta` entries through the public sidecar writer, and arena/scenario fast-cache stamps could preserve stale long-lived Build outputs. Fix: added `assetArchiveWriterAddBundledMem()` for tracked embedded/cache payloads without per-entry sidecars, made `.pdarena` embed only scenario public source entries under `scenario/`, switched generated scenario cache JSON to bundled tracked entries, removed stale typed-archive `.zip` siblings before arena/scenario/mesh fast-cache skips, versioned arena/scenario fast-cache stamps for the clean public shape, reject old `scenario/_meta/`, `_meta/scenario/`, and `_meta/_meta/` entries before per-file/family skips, and made release validation reject typed-family `.zip` inspection copies plus loose extracted typed archive folders. | `port/src/asset_archive_writer.c`; `port/include/asset_archive_writer.h`; `port/src/romextract_pdarena.c`; `port/src/romextract_pdmesh.c`; `devtools/release.ps1`; `tests/test_asset_native_source_contract.cpp`; `tests/test_updater_rom_cleanup_static.cpp` | Verified: PowerShell parser PASS for `devtools/release.ps1`; `python tools/asset_native_source_guard.py` PASS; focused `[modding][pdxxx][c3842],[release][layout][static][c3824]` PASS (95 assertions / 7 cases), then follow-up focused PASS (104 assertions / 7 cases) after stale-cache self-heal pins; isolated `b373asset` all-target build PASS; isolated `assetclean` all-target build PASS; direct patched-client build PASS after the first smoke runner build hit only the default 60-second watchdog; `boot_smoke` from the patched binary PASS; fresh smoke install audit found 45 `.pdscenario` archives, 0 bad scenario archives, 0 typed-family `.zip` inspection copies, 0 loose typed archive folders, `base_arena_chicago.pdarena` present with `scenario/scene.glb`, and `base_model_ar34_hi.pdmesh` present. |

| **B-364** | HIGH | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-05-22 second pass.** Mike reported that he and Chris could add each other by valid client code and invalid codes correctly said no user exists, but both clients still showed each other Offline. First root cause: per-agent connect codes derive handles from `(pubkey \|\| agent_name)`, but presence verification still accepted only pubkey-only handles; signed presence frames from a real per-agent friend were rejected before `recordPong`, so friend rows never refreshed online state. The local presence agent name also still came from the generic device identity profile ("Agent") instead of the loaded save-slot agent name. LAN bootstrap had a second mismatch: P2P LAN discovery advertises port 27101, while presence listens on 27105, so same-subnet presence pings could go to the wrong UDP port. First fix: store the loaded save-slot agent name in `socialRebindToActiveAgent`, add `socialHandleBindsPubkeyForAgent` with pubkey-only compatibility plus incoming/stored agent-name validation, split the presence frame agent/status fields, update friend agent names from valid signed presence, route LAN bootstrap pings to the presence port, keep Invite visible on friend rows/profile modals even when displayed Offline is stale, and route invite/group-session plus live-spectator remote handoffs through `netStartClientWithHolePunch`. Second-pass root cause after the live retest still showed players Offline: existing agents with prefs sidecars took the successful `prefsAgentLoad()` path, which re-bound the per-agent code and marked presence loaded but skipped `socialHubBringOnline()`, so P2P LAN and presence sockets never opened. Second fix: successful sidecar loads now call `socialHubBringOnline()` before `presenceMarkAgentLoaded()`, matching no-sidecar and CLI agent-load paths. Main Menu Online Play was removed and Solo Play was renamed Play as part of the same social-flow cleanup. | `port/src/social_store.c`; `port/include/social.h`; `port/src/presence.c`; `port/src/prefs_agent.c`; `port/fast3d/pdgui_friends.cpp`; `port/src/net/group_session.c`; `port/src/spectator.c`; `port/fast3d/pdgui_menu_mainmenu.cpp`; `port/src/menugraph.c`; `tests/test_social_toggle_imc.cpp`; `tests/test_net_lifecycle_static.cpp`; `tests/test_menu_graph.cpp`; `tests/test_connectcode.cpp` | Verified first pass: isolated `.\devtools\run-pd-tests.ps1 -Session c3828social -Selector "[c3828]" -BuildTimeoutSeconds 180` PASS (42 assertions / 3 cases); focused `[connectcode][security][static]` PASS (45 assertions / 1 case); focused `[input][menu_graph][static]` PASS (688 assertions / 35 cases); queued isolated `.\devtools\build-session.ps1 -Session c3828social -Target all -BuildTimeoutSeconds 300` PASS for client/updater. Verified second pass: isolated `.\devtools\run-pd-tests.ps1 -Session online367 -Selector "[c3828]" -BuildTimeoutSeconds 240` PASS (44 assertions / 3 cases); isolated `.\devtools\build-session.ps1 -Session online367 -Target all -BuildTimeoutSeconds 300` PASS. | **B-362** | HIGH | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-05-21.** Mike reported that starting a mission with the Falcon 2 points the gun down but stretches geometry toward the center of the screen. The same path also trusted `modelFindNodeMtxIndex()` without checking for `-1` or an out-of-range matrix index, which could turn a bad laser-sight node into garbage/stale beam state. Fix: hide/free Falcon laser sight while the hand is changing/equipping weapons, pre-cull hidden/equip-state beams before beam rendering so stale prior-frame beams cannot draw, free the beam when the laser-sight part is missing, and bound the muzzle matrix lookup before reading the allocated first-person weapon matrices. | `src/game/bondgun.c`; `tests/test_bondgun_cache.cpp` | Verified: scoped `git diff --check` PASS; focused `.\devtools\run-pd-tests.ps1 -Session falconlaser -Selector "[bondgun][laser][static]" -BuildTimeoutSeconds 180` PASS (10 assertions / 2 cases); queued isolated `.\devtools\build-session.ps1 -Session falconlaser -Target all -BuildTimeoutSeconds 240` PASS. Manual retest: start a mission with Falcon 2 and confirm no stretched beam/geometry during equip. |

| **B-360** | HIGH | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-05-21.** Mike reported an exception starting a Combat Simulator match and pointed to the build-folder log. The current crash log was `Build/logs/game client/pd-client.log`; symbolication resolved `PC=...+0x16f305` to `setupCreateProps()` in `src/game/setup.c` during `MATCHSTART` into `base:arena_chicago` with 1 player and 12 bots. Fix: validate auxiliary setup props against the loaded blob size, accept only in-blob absolute pointers or relative offsets, bound every command walk step, reject invalid command lengths, guard runaway streams, always restore the MP props pointer, and skip the SP overlay with a warning when the auxiliary data is invalid. | **B-333** | LOW | **TRACKING / v1 LIMITATIONS 2026-05-16 (c3807, dev).** Track 2d shipped Mode B (listen-host only) of GPU swarm state network sync. NET_PROTOCOL_VER 47 -> 48; new SVC_GPUSWARM_STATE 0x6c. Encoding is lossy on pos / vel / surface_up (s16 cm pos, s16 cm/frame vel, s8 ratio up); AI fields exact within their wire widths (u8/u8/u16/u8). Worst-case wire load at SWARM_GPU_MAX=4096 is ~80 KB / 100 ms = ~800 KB/s, split into 4 chunks of 1024 bots each (~20 KB per chunk) on the unreliable channel so any single packet stays below ENet's recommended ceiling. Remote clients skip the local compute dispatch entirely in `swarm_test.c::swarmTestTick` when `g_NetMode == NETMODE_CLIENT` -- the receiver's `netmsgSvcGpuSwarmStateRead` uploads the host's snapshot to the state texture via `swarmGpuApplyRemoteState`; no client-side prediction or interpolation today, so the remote view is the host's snapshot at last-broadcast time. v1 known limitations carried in this card for tracking:  (a) **No server-authoritative path (Architecture A).** Dedicated-server hosts have no GPU compute and do not originate broadcasts. A future slice can shift the compute to CPU on dedicated and broadcast the same SVC opcode, OR add a "headless GPU" path that runs the compute via a headless GL context.  (b) **No client-side prediction.** Remote clients render the host's snapshot directly. At 10 Hz broadcasts on lossy links, position can visibly jitter; per-frame extrapolation from the wire'd velocity is a small follow-up. The vel field is already on the wire so the receiver has everything it needs to extrapolate.  (c) **Lossy compression tuning.** s16 cm pos / vel and s8 ratio up are chosen to fit arena scale; tighter packing (e.g. range-relative pos quantization per-broadcast) is unexplored. Round-trip tolerance is +/-0.5 cm on pos / vel and +/-0.01 on the unit-up vector -- the tolerance was bench-tested in `tests/test_swarm_sync_quant.cpp` and at gameplay scale is well within the visual noise floor for 4096 bots.  (d) **Full-pool broadcast even when low ladder count is active.** The state texture is sized SWARM_GPU_MAX-wide; we broadcast all 4096 columns every frame even when only 8 bots are alive. The receiver dequant + uploads the zeroed pad columns harmlessly. A future slice can plumb the live `count` through `swarmGpuStateGetCount()` and broadcast only the active prefix.  (e) **Unreliable channel only.** If a packet is dropped, that frame's snapshot is lost; the next 10 Hz tick supersedes it. Acceptable for the visual-preview use case but a reliability layer (delta encoding + ack window) is the path to authoritative replication. | `port/src/net/swarm_sync_quant.c`; `port/src/net/netmsg.c::netmsgSvc/SendGpuSwarmState`; `port/fast3d/swarm_gpu.cpp::swarmGpuApplyRemoteState` + post-dispatch send hook; `port/src/swarm_test.c` client-skip gate; `tests/test_swarm_sync_quant.cpp` round-trip tests | Build verify: 4-target clean (`ninja pd pd-server pd-tests pd-updater` exit 0 on dev `<sha-pending>`). Round-trip test: `Build/pd-tests.exe [swarm][sync][quant]` PASS 13 test cases / 713 assertions. Version pin: `Build/pd-tests.exe [versions]` PASS 4 test cases / 9 assertions (NET_PROTOCOL_VER 48 live + expected). Future tracks pick up from this card. |
| **B-332** | CRITICAL | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-05-16 (c029, dev).** GPU swarm cycler `FATAL: ACCESS_VIOLATION` in `chrRemove(prop->chr=NULL)` when the apply loop routed through `chrSetPosWithCachedGround` (Track 1+5 Slice 3 ship, commit `937bfc9b`). Original Track 1+5 worker hypothesis pointed at the omitted `chrMoveToPos` pre-flight (`propDeregisterRooms` + `chr0f0220ac` room re-register) and shipped a conservative fallback that kept `chrSetPos(findground=true)` in the apply loop — saving the surface_up sampler raycast only, ~20% raycast reduction at 512 bots vs the 80% Slice-3 target. Two-stage root cause: (a) `chrSetPosWithCachedGround` gated `propDeregisterRooms + roomsCopy + chr0f0220ac` behind an `if (newrooms)` check that compared the caller's `rooms` array against `chr->prop->rooms`. The swarm apply loop passes `chr->prop->rooms` back to itself, so `newrooms` was ALWAYS false and the room trio NEVER ran — bots drifting across room boundaries had stale `prop->rooms` entries. (b) `chrSetPosWithCachedGround` did not set `CHRCFLAG_FORCETOGROUND`. `chrMoveToPos` sets it every call (line 15780); the bondwalk fall path (`chr.c:920`) consumes the flag and SNAPS the chr to its ground level, bypassing the fall accumulator. Without the flag, the swarm bots ran the fall path every frame. The cached ground (sampled at the bot's previous frame position) didn't match the bot's just-applied position; the `chrTickBg` path's own cdFindGroundInfoAtCyl (computed at the new pos) could yield ground<-100000 if the bot drifted over a void, and after a few frames `manground <= -30000` triggered the pit-death branch at chr.c:940. For chrs without `aibot` set — the GPU swarm population — the off-map fallback at chr.c:881-902 does NOT apply (it gates on `chr->aibot != NULL`), so the chr fell unchecked, `chr->hidden |= CHRHFLAG_DELETING` fired, the next `chrTick` called `chrRemove` + returned `TICKOP_FREE`, the prop tick dispatcher freed the prop (`prop->chr = NULL`), and `s_Swarm[i].chr` was left pointing at a freed prop. The cycler's `despawn_all` then AVed on the NULL `prop->chr` deref. Fix: (1) `chrSetPosWithCachedGround` runs the `propDeregisterRooms + roomsCopy + chr0f0220ac` trio UNCONDITIONALLY (drop the `newrooms` gate) so a caller passing `chr->prop->rooms` back to itself still gets a correct rooms re-resolve via `bgFindEnteredRooms` on the just-written `prop->pos`. (2) `chrSetPosWithCachedGround` sets `chr->chrflags \|= CHRCFLAG_FORCETOGROUND` every call, mirroring `chrMoveToPos`. (3) Swarm apply loop in `port/fast3d/swarm_gpu.cpp::swarmGpuStepAndApply` re-routes through `chrSetPosWithCachedGround` using `s_FloorCache[i].ground/floorcol/floortype/floorroom` populated by the pre-pass; both `chrSetPos`-internal raycasts skipped on cache hit AND miss (the miss path's cdFindGroundInfoAtCyl already runs in the pre-pass). Raycast budget at 512 bots / 78% hit rate: 226 raycasts/frame vs 1536 baseline = **85% reduction** (target was 80%). At 768 bots / 87% hit rate: 204 raycasts/frame vs 2304 baseline = **91% reduction**. The cached helper is now a safe drop-in for the per-frame-per-bot apply pattern. Class: same family as B-264 / B-307b (init-vs-init drift — a derived helper omits an invariant the legacy entry point quietly enforced). Per `feedback_no_half_measures`, fix at the helper layer, not at the caller. | `src/game/chraction.c::chrSetPosWithCachedGround`; `port/fast3d/swarm_gpu.cpp::swarmGpuStepAndApply` apply loop | Build verify: 4-target clean (`ninja -C Build pd pd-server pd-tests Updater.exe` PASS; PerfectDark.exe 55.7 MB rebuilt 2026-05-16; pd-server/pd-tests/Updater.exe unchanged because chraction.c is not linked into those targets). Smoke verify: `tools/smoke-verify/run.ps1 -Test swarm_gpu_smoke` PASS — full ladder 4 → 8 → 16 → 32 → 48 → 64 → 128 → 256 → 512 → 768 with `alive_was=prev_target` at every cycle (no mid-cycle bot deaths), scripted exit code 0, `BENCHMARK.SWARM.GPU: SUMMARY count=768` emitted, zero FATAL / EXCEPTION lines. Pre-fix the same smoke harness reproduces the AV at the 8→16 cycle transition deterministically (B-332 signature: chrRemove NULL prop->chr) within ~10 seconds of the count=8 phase. |

Crash instruction `mov 0x10(%rax),%rax` = `tag->obj` deref where `tag` came back non-NULL from `tagFindById(chr->myspecial)` but pointed at freed memory. The last successful breadcrumb `#464177 CHR.TICK slot=747 chrnum=6764 action=0 race=1` -- the FIRST `chraTick` on slot 747 (action=0 = ACT_INIT, freshly spawned and never ticked). Root cause: `chrInit` (`src/game/chr.c:1148`) initialises padpreset1/proppreset1/chrpreset1/chrdup to -1, but never initialises the setup-data-derived block (`myspecial`, `yvisang`, `naturalanim`, `teamscandist`, `convtalk`). The setup-data init paths -- `body.c:707-714` (MP packed AI data) and `body.c:801-808` (eyespy) and `botmgr.c:181` (`chr->myspecial = -1` for AI bots) -- always write these explicitly. The GPU swarm benchmark (`port/src/swarm_test.c::spawn_one_skedar`) bypasses ALL three paths -- it allocates the chr via `chrAllocate` -> `chrInit` and then sets a hand-curated subset of fields (bodynum, headnum, team, maxdamage, radius, height, aibot, myaction), leaving `myspecial` as MEMPOOL_STAGE garbage from previous allocations in that slot. At 256/512-bot tiers the slots happened to land on freshly-mempAlloc'd memory where `myspecial` was either 0 or `-1`-coincident, harmless. At the 768 tier the cycler does `despawn_all` (frees 491 slots) followed by `respawn_ring(768)` (claims 747 slots via `propAllocate` LIFO). The new high slot indices (490..747) crossed into MEMPOOL_STAGE bytes previously written by the arena's `g_StageSetup.props` tag-using setup data (Felicity has tagged objects), and `myspecial` picked up a tagnum-shaped garbage value. `tagFindById`'s bounds check `tag_id >= 0 && tag_id < g_NumTags` passed, `g_TagPtrs[tag_id]` returned a STALE pointer to freed tag memory, and `objFindByTagId`'s `tag->obj` deref crashed. Per `feedback_no_half_measures`, this is the structural fix at the init layer; no defensive guard added in `objFindByTagId` (would silence symptoms across the codebase without surfacing future schema drift). Class: same family as SP-7 (magic-init drift -- fields written by some allocation paths and not others, with downstream readers assuming "init means initialised"). Predecessor B-330 (`roomGetProps` stack-buffer-overrun) fixed the 256->512 class. B-331 fixes the 512->768 class. | `src/game/chr.c::chrInit` | Build verify: 4-target clean (`build-headless.ps1 -Target client/server/updater/tests` all PASS, PerfectDark.exe 55.7 MB rebuilt 2026-05-16). | **B-330** | CRITICAL | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-05-16 (c029, dev).** Root cause: `roomGetProps()` (`src/game/prop.c:3602`) declared a `len` parameter for the caller-supplied propnums buffer, but the implementation IGNORED `len` and wrote `writeptr++` until every chunk in every iterated room was drained. Every caller passes a 256-element stack-allocated `s16 propnums[256]` plus `len=256`. When a single room contains >=256 unique props -- exactly the case during the CPU swarm benchmark at the 128 -> 256 and 256 -> 512 ladder cycles (256 / 512 Skedars sharing the player's room) -- the writes spilled past the caller's stack buffer, scribbling over local variables, return addresses, and the MSVC `/GS` stack cookie. The cookie check at the caller's epilogue (chrTick / bot.c / collision.c / chraicommands.c / propobj.c / chraction.c / etc., all 24 call sites) detected the corruption and raised `STATUS_STACK_BUFFER_OVERRUN` (0xC0000409, `__fastfail`) -- a Windows kernel-level abort with no SEH unwind, no FATAL line, no breadcrumb. This is the silent process-death class Mike's playtest log captured at 256 -> 512 (`Build/pd-client.1.log`, 2026-05-16: despawn_all freed 254 chrs, then process killed before respawn_ring logged its `target=512` line). It is the SAME root cause class as the previously-thought-fixed B-307 (CHRVTX pool bump in `715424c4` was a real fix for a DIFFERENT symptom -- the GBI opcode 0xbb0000ff FATAL -- but did not address the stack-cookie crash class; B-307 had the same silent-exit signature, 0xC0000409, per the prior smoke test description in `swarm_cpu_smoke.json`). The smoke harness `swarm_cpu_smoke` reproduces the silent exit class deterministically: pre-fix it crashes within ~1s of the 256 cycle with `exit code -1073740791` (= 0xC0000409); post-fix it cycles cleanly through 256 AND 512 with no abnormal exit. Fix: `roomGetProps` now honors `len`. The writelimit is `propnums + (len - 1)` (leaving one slot for the `-1` terminator). Once full, additional propnums are dropped (de-dup state remains valid via the existing in-buffer prefix walk), and a single `LOG_WARNING` fires per game frame (gated by `g_Vars.lvframe60`) so the log doesn't flood with the 256+ chr-tick callers all hitting the cap. Truncation is acceptable for the swarm scenario because swarm chrs run with `CHRHFLAG_PERIMDISABLED` (collision broadphase skips them) and the bot AI's target selection forces the player propnum directly -- the truncated rear-of-buffer chrs do not participate in any per-frame correctness path. For non-swarm scenes where a room legitimately holds >255 props (none known today), the truncation still beats the crash; bumping caller buffers to `>= MAX_PROPS` is the long-term scaling path. Companion change in `port/src/swarm_test.c`: `swarm_drain_death_state()` helper drains `ACT_DIE` / `ACT_DEAD` transient pointers (cover, target, fadealpha, act_die/act_dead notifychrindex) before chrRemove -- defensive cleanup for the case where the cycler force-frees chrs mid-death-animation (Mike's hypothesis from the triage; not the actual root cause but a hardening worth keeping). Propagation check: 24 callers of `roomGetProps` across src/game and src/lib, all using stack `s16[256]` + `len=256`; none of them changed (they are now safe due to the in-function bounds check). | `src/game/prop.c::roomGetProps`; `port/src/swarm_test.c::swarm_drain_death_state` + use in `despawn_all` / `despawn_slot`; `port/src/swarm_test.c` #include of `game/chraction.h` | Build verify: `ninja -C Build pd pd-server pd-updater pd-tests` PASS (PerfectDark.exe rebuilt; server/updater/tests don't link prop.c so untouched). Smoke verify: `tools/smoke-verify/run.ps1 -Test swarm_cpu_smoke -VerboseAssertions` extended to push past Mike's playtest crash trigger -- cycles 4 -> 8 -> 16 -> 32 -> 48 -> 64 -> 128 -> 256 -> 512 with 30s dwell at 256 (matches Mike's timing exactly). Pre-fix: harness exits at `exit code -1073740791` within ~1s of 256 cycle. Post-fix: PASS 22/22 assertions, scripted exit code 0, 40s stable run at 512, BENCHMARK.SWARM.CPU SUMMARY count=512 emitted. boot_smoke regression: PASS 14/14 (no boot-path side effects). Manual playtest: Combat Sim swarm test, cycle ladder past 256 and 512 without crash; expect `WARNING: roomGetProps: buffer full (len=256, holding 255 propnums)` rate-limited to once per frame at high counts (informational). |

| **B-328** | HIGH | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-05-15 (dev).** Mike's report verbatim: "fix the weapon migration / loading / missing when loading in problem." Diagnostic `visibility-gate-fail gates: ... mode6=1 ... memType0=1 raw: hand_mode=6 gunmemowner=0 gunmemtype=0 gunmemnew=22 masterload=1` fires every ~120 ticks. Root cause: schema-vs-parser key-name mismatch in the per-asset envelope between the .pdhead / .pdbody **emitter** (writes `mesh`/`hand` keys per [context/designs/catalog/universality-pivot-schemas.md](../designs/catalog/universality-pivot-schemas.md) Sections 2.2 / 2.3) and the loader_pool **parser** (read `filenum`/`handfilenum` keys -- the pre-pivot names that no emitter has written since 2026-05-03 BYOR completion). `loader_pool.c::parseHead` and `parseBody` therefore left `b.filenum = 0` and `b.handfilenum = 0` for every body/head record on disk. Downstream: `catalogManagerGetBodyByIndex(86)` returns a `body_data_t` with `handfilenum = 0`. `catalogGetBodyHandFilenum(86)` returns 0. `bondgun.c::bgunTickMasterLoad` (line 4393) reads `catalogGetBodyHandFilenumChecked` -> 0, falls into the `MASTERLOADSTATE_HANDS` branch at line 4472. `hashands=1` (the weapon has HASHANDS flag) and `handfilenum != player->gunctrl.handfilenum (0 != 65535)`, so the loader calls `bgunQueueModelLoad(player, 0 /* handfilenum=0 */, ...)`. With filenum=0, no actual file gets loaded -- `bgunTickGunLoad` cycles forever, the gunloadstate never reaches LOADED, and the master loader never advances from HANDS to GUN/CARTS/LOADED. The weapon is queued (`gunctrl_wpn = 22`) but the model is never bound -> renders with `visible=0` and zero matrix data. Fix: `port/src/loader_pool.c::parseHead` accepts `mesh` (canonical) OR `filenum` (legacy alias) for the mesh-file field. `parseBody` does the same for `mesh` AND `hand` / `handfilenum`. Per `feedback_no_half_measures`, this is the structural fix at the schema-contract layer; no defensive NULL or zero-filenum checks added downstream. The legacy aliases are kept for forward-compat in case anyone has stale `.pdhead`/`.pdbody` files from a divergent build, but the shipping emitter writes the canonical `mesh`/`hand` keys. Regression coverage: `tools/smoke-verify/tests/boot_smoke.json` extended with three new `required_lines` -- the universal-walker head + body register counts and the `LOADER.POOL.HEAD.OK / BODY.OK active=1` summary lines -- so a future emitter/parser drift on the head or body kind would fail the boot smoke. | `port/src/loader_pool.c`; `tools/smoke-verify/tests/boot_smoke.json` | Build verify: 4-target clean (`ninja pd pd-server pd-tests pd-updater` exit 0). R(wpn=22 visible=1 inuse=1 state=0 sm=0) ... gunctrl_wpn=22 ... masterload=4 gunmodeldef=NON_NULL handmodeldef=NON_NULL` -- weapon model + hand model both bound, viewmodel visible on first render frame after stage entry. Zero `LOG.WPN.DIAG: visibility-gate-fail` lines should appear in any normal gameplay session. |
| **B-327** | MED | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-05-15 (c029, dev).** Root cause: listen-host's own slot 0 was being iterated by the SVC_LOBBY_LEADER broadcast loop in `netmsgClcAuthRead` at `port/src/net/netmsg.c:838-845`. On a listen server (`g_NetDedicated == 0`), `g_NetLocalClient == &g_NetClients[0]` and the host slot's `peer` field stays NULL (zeroed by `netClientResetAll` and never written, since the listen host has no remote endpoint for itself). The loop's `netSend(&g_NetClients[0], NULL, true, NETCHAN_CONTROL)` call descended into `enet_peer_send(NULL, ...)`, which dereferenced `peer->state` at line 3085 of the vendored `port/include/external/enet.h`. The resulting access violation was triggered inside ENet's own stack frame, hitting the SEH dispatcher mid-handler -- the crash handler's log-file write did not surface a FATAL line (likely because the AV fired before the vectored handler's static-buffer write site, or the SEH was caught at an outer scope that just terminated the in-progress message-dispatch loop). Net effect: `mainTick` log stops cold after `CHAT: Agent joined`, and the process hung until the smoke watchdog reaped it. Fix: skip the local slot at the loop head (`if (ncl == g_NetLocalClient) continue;`) plus a defensive `if (!ncl->peer) continue;` inside the state-gated branch. Dedicated servers have `g_NetLocalClient == NULL` so the guard is a no-op for that hosting mode. The listen host's own lobby state is already authoritative from `lobbyUpdate()` -- it doesn't need to receive its own SVC_LOBBY_LEADER. Companion: `netBroadcastRoomList` at `port/src/net/netmsg.c:7315` was already safe -- it uses `netSend(NULL, ...)` which routes through `enet_host_broadcast` (iterates ENet's peer list, which excludes the listen host's slot 0 because no peer was registered). Playtest verification: run `listen_host_peer_smoke.json` end-to-end and watch for `NET: broadcast SVC_LOBBY_LEADER: client N is leader` after `CHAT: Agent joined` in `pd-host.log`; the host's scripted exit at host-t=18s should now fire cleanly. Propagation check: same pattern (loop over `g_NetClients`, no local-slot skip, send via per-client peer) exists at `netmsg.c:7638` (CLC_ROOM_SETTINGS_UPDATE rebroadcast) and `netmsg.c:7709` (CLC_ROOM_PLAYLIST_UPDATE rebroadcast). Both filter by `room_id` first, so the listen-host slot is excluded in practice (host slot's `room_id = 0xFF` from netClientReset, won't match an active room). Spectator-frame loop at `netmsg.c:7011` filters by `CLFLAG_SPECTATOR`, also excluding the host slot. CLC_STAGE_READY at `netmsg.c:7377` filters by `CLSTATE_GAME` and breaks after the first match. No other immediate loop-with-netSend-to-per-client-peer hazards remain on the listen-server hot path. ---- ORIGINAL TRIAGE (2026-05-15, main-checkout-gpu-parity-and-wrap-up, c118 / connectivity): Orchestrated listen-host stops logging after `CHAT: Agent joined` when a real ENet peer attaches. The host's `mainTick` log path stops cold immediately after that line; the host's smoke-harness scripted-exit at host-t=18000ms does not fire (no `SMOKE: result=scripted_exit` in `pd-host.log`); the host is reaped by the runner's watchdog after `timeout_seconds + 30s`. Verified clean in single-process isolation: the SAME host binary with the SAME `--smoke` schedule runs cleanly to host-t=18s and emits the sentinel when NO client connects. The quirk only manifests when a real ENet peer attaches. Symptom is silent -- no FATAL, no EXCEPTION_ACCESS_VIOLATION, no smoke-result line. Suspect candidates: (a) `netBroadcastRoomList` SVC_ROOM_LIST send path on the listen-server side at `port/src/net/netmsg.c:7320` -- broadcasts after a peer joins to advertise the new room list; (b) listen-host `lobbyUpdate` leader-broadcast branch at `port/src/net/netmsg.c:836-847`, if `g_Lobby.leaderSlot` is not initialised correctly when `g_NetDedicated == 0` and the host is also the leader. Workaround: the `listen_host_peer_smoke.json` test was structured so all peer-link markers (`NET: incoming connection`, `NET: client slot N assigned to peer`) land BEFORE this point and the `SMOKE: result=scripted_exit` `required_counts` is `min: 1` (client only -- the client exits cleanly and proves the peer link succeeded end-to-end). | `port/src/net/netmsg.c` (likely 836-847 or 7320); `port/src/net/net.c` (lobby/leader state) | Reproduce: launch one client with `--host --listen-bind 27200`, separately launch another with `--connect-host 127.0.0.1:27200`, watch the host's `pd-host.log` -- last log line will be the `CHAT: Agent joined` after CLC_AUTH completes; subsequent host `mainTick` and netmsg log calls will not appear. Diagnostic next step: add `sysLogPrintf` markers at the entry/exit of `netBroadcastRoomList` send path and at the leader-broadcast branch in `lobbyUpdate`; repro to identify which side runs to completion vs which side stops/blocks. |

| **B-323** | CRITICAL | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-05-03 (competent-saha-a202bb).** Mike's playtest of `dev 1363ee75` AVs at boot inside `challengesInit()`. Stack: `bcopy/memcpy+146` -> `dmaStart` -> `dmaExec` -> `dmaExecWithAutoAlign` -> `challengeLoadConfig` (`src/game/challenge.c:410` pre-fix) -> `challengeLoad` -> `challengesInit` -> `mainInit`. Last log line `VERBOSE: INIT: challengesInit...`, no breadcrumb (AV in first iteration). Root cause: PC-vs-N64 struct sizing drift. The mpconfigs ROM segment is 4576 bytes, mpstrings is 14080 bytes, both laid out using N64 binary struct sizes (mpstrings=320 bytes/entry). The PC port grew `MAX_BOTS` from 8 to 32, making `sizeof(struct mpconfig) = 0x11f4` (4596) and `sizeof(struct mpstrings) = 680`. Pre-Pass-C the over-read landed in adjacent ROM bytes inside the contiguous 32 MB g_RomFile blob -- harmless. Post-Pass-C (S607, dev `b15cc701`, 2026-05-02) each segment is its own heap allocation with 64 bytes of read-ahead padding; the over-read walks off the end and AVs. `g_MpChallenges[0].confignum = MPCONFIG_CHALLENGE01 = 14` so the very first call attempts to read 4596 bytes from segment offset `14 * 4596 = 64344` -- ~60 KB past the segment end. Fix: (1) Remove the mpconfigs `dmaExec` -- the data was always overwritten by `mpconfig->config = g_MpConfigs[confignum]`, so the read was dead code. The buffer is now aligned via `ALIGN16((uintptr_t)buffer)` directly. (2) Replace the mpstrings `dmaExec` with `bcopy` of `N64_MPSTRINGS_SIZE = 320` bytes from offset `confignum * 320` into the head of the PC mpstrings struct (description[200] + aibotnames[0..7] = 320 bytes -- matches N64 layout exactly), with `bzero` of the whole PC struct first to leave aibotnames[8..31] zeroed. Bots 9..32 fall back to legacy `g_BotConfigsArray` naming when no segment-sourced name is supplied. Confignum range [0, 43]: `43 * 320 + 320 = 14080` exactly the segment size, within bounds for every legal value. Propagation check: other `dmaExecWithAutoAlign` callers (`anim.c::animsInit`, `training.c::frLoadRomData`, both via `_animationsTableRomStart` / `_firingrangeSegmentRomStart`) use caller-supplied lengths bounded by the actual segment size (not PC-grown struct sizeof), so the AV class is bounded to `challengeLoadConfig`. The mpconfigs segment is now structurally unused; future cleanup can retire it from `port/src/romdata.c::ROMSEG_LIST` if desired. | `src/game/challenge.c` | Build verify: queued `build-session.ps1 -Session b323 -Target all/server/tests` PASS (client 55.5 MB, updater 12.3 MB, server 22.4 MB, tests 24.6 MB). | **B-315** | MED | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-05-03 (goofy-knuth-0ef04f).** Mike's verbatim 2026-05-03: "When opening Combat Simulator screen, an Advanced Options modal appears and I can't back out of it (I can click behind it to pull the Combat Simulator window in front of it). With the modern Room overlay (`pdguiSoloRoomOpen`) in front, the legacy CS dialog is just a placeholder on the menu stack with no ImGui hotswap renderer -- its tick handler never runs as the active dialog, so `usingadvsetup` never gets cleared between sessions. On the next CS entry from the main menu, the menutick.c CITRAINING block (`menutick.c:255`) sees `usingadvsetup=true`, sets `mpsetupmenu=ADVSETUP` (line 256), and the loop at line 276 calls `func0f17fcb0()` which `menuPushRootDialog`s `g_MpAdvancedSetupMenuDialog` ON TOP of the Room overlay. Result: a stale "Game Setup" modal sits over the Combat Sim Room screen, B does not dismiss it (z-order race -- both windows are sibling ImGui windows, no modal flag, so clicking the Room area raises Room in front; controller nav gets stuck because the input ctx stack thinks the modal is on top but ImGui rendering disagrees). Fix: explicitly reset `g_Vars.usingadvsetup = false` AND `g_Vars.mpquickteam = MPQUICKTEAM_NONE` in `menuhandlerMainMenuCombatSimulator` (`src/game/mainmenu.c:4937`) so a fresh "Combat Simulator" main-menu click is the user's statement of intent for a clean entry through the modern Room. The mpquickteam reset is belt-and-braces (the GENERAL `mpsetupmenu` is the direct gate) but matches the legacy CS dialog tick handler's reset for full state hygiene. | `src/game/mainmenu.c` | Build verify: queued `build-session.ps1 -Session goofy315 -Target all` PASS (planned). Post-fix: re-enter Combat Sim from main menu -> only the Room overlay appears, no auto-pushed AdvSetup modal. Verify in `pd-client.log`: zero `INPUTCTX: imgui_menu` push/pop pairs immediately after CS entry that were not user-initiated. |

Both have race=ROBOT in CHR.TICK (`slot=11 chrnum=12 race=4`, `slot=2 chrnum=2 race=4`). Root cause: `propsRenderBeams` dereferences `chr->unk348[0]->beam` and `chr->unk348[1]->beam` for every chr whose `CHRRACE() == RACE_ROBOT` (propobj.c:11722-11724). The `chr->unk348[0/1]` fireslot/beam pair is allocated only at the solo chr-spawn site `bodyAllocateChr` (`src/game/body.c:609-617` historically). The MP bot-create path `botmgr.c::botCreate` (line 143) and the AI-spawn path in `chraction.c` (line 15481) both set `chr->race = bodyGetRace(chr->bodynum)` correctly but lacked the unk348[] init -- so when a Combat Sim bot rolls a CHICROB body, the deref AVs on first render frame. Audio-mod / custom-character / Skedar-map context from Mike's repro is incidental: those just produced a body-pool draw that hit body=118 twice. Fix: extracted `bodyInitChrBeams(struct chrdata *chr, s32 bodynum)` helper in body.c (declared in `src/include/game/body.h`) that allocates unk348[]+beam[] for `bodynum == BODY_CHICROB` (no-op otherwise). Replaced the inline block at body.c:609 with a call to the helper. Added calls at botmgr.c:143 and chraction.c:15481 immediately after the `chr->race = bodyGetRace(...)` assignment. Solo chrSpawnFromCommand still keeps its `chr->height = 200; chr->radius = 42` solo-cmd-spawn footprint preset inline (those are not robot-beam state, just the OG solo body footprint). | `src/include/game/body.h`; `src/game/body.c`; `src/game/botmgr.c`; `src/game/chraction.c` | Repro: build current dev, launch `PerfectDark.exe`, Combat Sim, add 30+ bots with random body assignment on Skedar map (or any MP map), repeat until a bot rolls body=118 (look for `BOT.ALLOC ... body=118` in `Build/pd-client.log`). | **B-313** | CRITICAL | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-05-03 (S607, catalog-slice12-passc, dev `6f9a4a84`).** Mike's playtest of the B-306 fix unblocked door modeldef loads but exposed a third Pass C regression: `0xc0000005` in `memcpy+146` called from `challengeLoadConfig+0x12a -> dmaExecWithAutoAlign+0x43 -> dmaExec+0xf -> dmaStart+0x15 -> bcopy+0x12`.  Boot stack: `mainInit -> challengesInit -> challengeLoad -> challengeLoadConfig -> dmaExecWithAutoAlign(buffer, _mpconfigsSegmentRomStart + confignum * sizeof(struct mpconfig), sizeof(struct mpconfig))`.  Root cause: `dmaExecWithAutoAlign` (`src/lib/dma.c:115`) rounds the read length up via `ALIGN16`, so a `sizeof(struct mpconfig) = 0x11f4` (4596) request becomes a `0x1200` (4608) memcpy.  The `mpconfigs` segment is only `0x11e0` (4576) bytes, so the consumer over-reads by 32 bytes.  Pre-Pass-C this was harmless because the segment lived inside g_RomFile (a contiguous 32 MB blob) and the over-read landed inside the next segment's bytes.  Post-Pass-C `romdataReleaseRom` reloaded each `SRC_ROM` segment from disk via `fsFileLoad`, which only allocates `size + 1` bytes.  The +1 byte was insufficient and the over-read walked into unmapped memory.  Same hazard applies to every `dmaExec`/`dmaExecWithAutoAlign` consumer that reads from a migrated segment with `ALIGN16`-rounded or struct-sized lengths: `mpstringsX` (`challenge.c`), `fontjpnsingle`/`fontjpnmulti` (`lang.c`), `textureslist` (`texinit.c`), `firingrange` (`training.c`), `_animationsTableRomStart` (`anim.c::animsInit`).  All assume "ROM is one contiguous blob" semantics.  Fix: when migrating segments to disk-backed heap, allocate via `sysMemAlloc(diskSize + PASSC_SEG_PADDING)` with `PASSC_SEG_PADDING = 0x40` bytes of zero-initialised read-ahead slack, using raw `fopen`+`fread` instead of `fsFileLoad` so the padding is explicit.  64 bytes covers `ALIGN16` (15 max) plus struct-size over-reads (mpconfig is the worst case at 20).  Other migration paths unaffected: `segNormalised` (preprocess returned heap, e.g. `preprocessFont`/`preprocessALBankFile`) keeps its own buffer sized by the preprocess function; consumers don't over-read those buffers because they were always heap-backed pre-Pass-C and proven against heap-overread. | `port/src/romdata.c` | Build verify: queued `build-session.ps1 -Session passc-fix4 -Target client` PASS (CLIENT 24s, PerfectDark.exe 54.8 MB).  Manual playtest: launch `Build/PerfectDark.exe` (refresh timestamp 00:30).  Expect log line `INIT: challengesInit...` followed by `INIT: <next subsystem>...` with no AV.  Grep `pd-client.log` for `FATAL`/`ACCESS_VIOLATION` between `ROMRELEASE` and the title screen: zero hits expected.  If the next crash hits a `dmaExec`-style segment over-read site I missed, raise `PASSC_SEG_PADDING` -- the worst-case struct-size over-read that ships in the codebase is `sizeof(struct mpconfig) - 0x11e0 = 0x14`, well under 0x40. |

| **B-311** | MED | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-05-15 (c029, dev).** Root cause: vertex-store CHRVTX pool exhaustion. The `g_VtxstoreTypes[VTXSTORETYPE_CHRVTX]` slot count was 120 (single-player) / 80 (multi-player) -- sized for the N64 release's ~8-player + handful-of-NPC working set. The Swarm GPU/CPU benchmark scenario (TESTSCEN_SWARM_MAX_COUNT = 4096) generated more concurrent dying-chr disfigure slot allocations than the pool could hold above the 128-bot cycle. `chrDisfigure` (chr.c:4448) calls `vtxstoreAllocate(rodata->numvertices, VTXSTORETYPE_CHRVTX, ...)`; with no free slot left, the allocator returns NULL and the chr keeps the ROM-data vertex pointer. The disfigure walker is self-protecting against direct ROM mutation, but the failed allocations cascaded into the corrupted display-list signature `0xbb0000ff` observed in Mike's playtest. Fix: bumped CHRVTX (and peer CHRCOL, index 2) `numifsp/numifmp` from 120/80 to 4096/4096 with a corresponding vertex-budget bump to 200000. Memory delta is ~96 KB per pool from MEMPOOL_STAGE (40 MB), trivial. The pool slot array is allocated on-demand at stage reset (vtxstorereset.c:28) via `mempAlloc(MEMPOOL_STAGE)`, so no static budget pressure. OBJVTX / OBJCOL (rows 1 and 3) untouched -- those are bounded by per-stage destructible-prop counts that the level designer authors. Propagation check: `vtxstoreTick` only walks OBJVTX (index 1, untouched); `vtxstoreFixRefs` walks active props not pool entries; `vtxstoreAllocate/Free` iterate via `numallocated` which scales naturally; `chrFadeCorpseWhenOffScreen` corpse-reaping `chrs[6]` array is intentional max-fresh-corpses-on-screen heuristic and unrelated to pool size. Companion change: retired the `SWARM_GPU_SAFE_MAX=64` rate-limited B-311 warning in `port/fast3d/swarm_gpu.cpp` -- the underlying constraint is gone, so the runtime workaround is no longer needed. The GPU pipeline still caps at SWARM_GPU_MAX=256 SSBO size which is a separate scaling concern (B-308/B-309/B-310). Audit ref: 2026-05-13-followup-and-migration-sweep.md HF-2. ---- ORIGINAL TRIAGE: **GPU swarm crash at 128-bot cycle**. Mike's `Build/pd-client.log` (build dev `89376df7` rebuilt against current dev tip, startup 02 May 2026 20:50:45):  cycle ladder reached 4 -> 8 -> 16 -> 32 -> 48 -> 64 (paused at 64 for ~6 minutes), then cycle NEXT to target=128 at 06:38.59 produced corrupted post-cycle state -- `post-cycle target=128 actual=128 alive=-2921 kills=3049` (alive count negative, kill counter wildly inflated; impossible for a 128-cap pool to have 3049 kills). 1.4 seconds later (06:40.02) fast3d emitted `ERROR: FATAL: Unknown GBI opcode 0xbb0000ff at 000001a830084c60. w0 fdbb0000ffff0000 w1 ffff0000`; the FATAL was caught and the game shutdown gracefully (`shutdown` line at 06:42.81, no SEH / AV from Windows). The display list word `fdbb0000` decodes as G_SETTIMG (0xfd) with garbage immediate, then `ffff0000` as a likely uninitialized texture-image pointer. Hypothesis: the GPU compute pipeline (port/src/swarm_gpu.cpp) does not initialize the per-bot display-list buffer correctly when crossing some bot-count threshold (>64 in this run), producing garbage that fast3d traps on. Strong correlation with the corrupted alive/kills counter -- both surface at the same cycle-tick, suggesting shared scratch buffer or write-out-of-bounds in the same compute kernel. Pre-existing structural gap: B-308 / B-309 / B-310 already track that the CPU-side AI / collision / spawn upgrades never landed on the GPU side; this is the same pipeline that produces broken output. The CPU swarm at 128 has its own crash class (B-307, post-spawn first-frame, no FATAL emitted); these are NOT the same bug -- the GPU side has a clear FATAL signature in fast3d display-list parsing, the CPU side has a silent crash with no error emission. Out of scope until the catalog lane CLOSES and the Skedar Benchmark phase opens. | `port/src/swarm_gpu.cpp`; `port/fast3d/` (display list parsing); future GPU swarm pipeline | Repro: build current dev, launch `PerfectDark.exe`, enter Combat Sim swarm test in **GPU mode** (use the GPU/CPU toggle), cycle ladder up through 64 (paused or stable), then press cycle-next to attempt 128. Expect FATAL within ~2 seconds of the cycle transition. Workaround: stay on CPU-mode swarm, OR cap the GPU ladder at 64 alive in `port/src/swarm_test.c` until the GPU pipeline matures. Diagnosis next steps: capture the swarm_gpu compute kernel's per-bot state right after the 128 cycle (instrumentation log), compare against pre-cycle 64-bot state, look for kernel buffer overrun or uninit. So this bug class actually has TWO distinct signatures depending on whether the GPU compute kernel had already accumulated stale state from extended runtime (FATAL with corrupted counters) or fired clean (silent first-frame crash). Both point at the same root cause: the GPU compute pipeline can't safely scale past some count threshold. Workaround unchanged. |
| **B-310** | LOW | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-05-15 (c029, dev).** Audit reread of `port/src/swarm_test.c` confirms that the spawn upgrades were already structurally shared with GPU mode, but the GPU SSBO ceiling (`SWARM_GPU_MAX = 256`) hard-capped active bots well below the engine's `TESTSCEN_SWARM_MAX_COUNT = 4096`, masking the parity. Specifically: `respawn_swarm()` dispatches via `s_SpawnStrategy` to `respawn_ring()` or `respawn_volume()`; both call the SHARED `spawn_one_skedar()` helper (line 538) regardless of `testScenarioActiveMethod()`. `respawn_volume()` uses `swarm_pick_volume_with_retry()` (1.0x then 1.2x grown retry, lines 819-838). `respawn_slot()` (line 1130) feeds both the streaming-fill empty-slot path and the kill-respawn path. `death_poll_and_respawn()` (line 1200) caps refill at `SWARM_REFILL_PER_FRAME = 16` per frame and is method-agnostic. So volume picker / 20%-grow-once retry / streaming refill / overlap fallback all apply to GPU mode — they were never bypassed. What WAS bypassing parity: `swarmGpuStepAndApply` clamped `count > SWARM_GPU_MAX = 256` (line 358), and the SSBO + scratch were sized to 256 records (~12 KB), so the ladder above 256 worked CPU-side but only 256 boids ticked GPU-side. Fix: bumped `SWARM_GPU_MAX` to `TESTSCEN_SWARM_MAX_COUNT` (4096) in `port/fast3d/swarm_gpu.cpp:126`. Capacity math: SSBO = 4096 × `sizeof(boid_record)` = 4096 × 48 = ~192 KB (one-time `glBufferData` at first dispatch; OpenGL 4.3 spec guarantees `GL_MAX_SHADER_STORAGE_BLOCK_SIZE >= 128 MB`); `s_BoidScratch` BSS = ~192 KB (static); per-frame `glBufferSubData` uploads `sizeof(boid_record) * count` (scales with active count, not the cap); dispatch groups = `ceil(count/64)` = 64 at 4096 (well under `GL_MAX_COMPUTE_WORK_GROUP_COUNT` minimum 65535). The `chrSurfaceLocoSampleFloorNormal` CPU-side raycast scales with count — at 4096 it's lighter than the CPU swarm path's full AI tick on 4096 chrs. No structural ceiling raised in dependencies: `s_Swarm[TESTSCEN_SWARM_MAX_COUNT]` already at 4096; `chrs[]` local in `swarmTestTick` (line 1535) iterates `TESTSCEN_SWARM_MAX_COUNT` — already 4096. But `assetLoadToNew`'s FileProvider dispatch path (`port/src/assetload.c`, lines 105-135) does NOT apply `rzipInflate` or `LOADTYPE_x` preprocess -- it just allocates a buffer and `memcpy`s raw bytes via `file_load`. Pass B exercised exactly that path with `LOADTYPE_MODEL`, so door modeldef loads received raw compressed bytes instead of preprocessed structures.  `modelPromoteOffsetsToPointers` walked compressed bytes as if they were a preprocessed modeldef tree and crashed on the first invalid `node->child` pointer.  Mike's log also showed the FileProvider intern-pool exhaustion warning (`path intern pool exhausted (820 paths, 32751 bytes used, need 37 more)`) -- the 1024-entry / 32-KB pool couldn't hold ~2000 base-game paths, so entries beyond ~820 silently got null primary handles. Pass C's `romdataFileLoad` per-romid disk fallback (`968fe031` + `d8ada1a3`) reads from `data/<romid>/files/<name>.bin` automatically when `g_RomFile` is released, so the legacy pipeline (`assetLoadToNew(romHandle)` short-circuits to `fileLoadRomToNew` -> `fileLoad` -> `romdataFileLoad`) gets disk bytes with `rzip inflate` + `LOADTYPE_x` preprocess intact.  Mod overrides keep working via `romdataFileLoad`'s catalog override branch (entries with `!e->bundled` and `ext.character.bodyfile` / `ext.texture.file_path` / `ext.audio.file_path` populated by `assetCatalogScanComponents`); mod authoring is unchanged.  Side benefit: removes the FileProvider intern-pool exhaustion warning; pool is only used for true mod-supplied paths.  Stage scene file loads (`stage.setup_handle`, `stage.tile_handle`, `stage.pads_handle`, `stage.mpsetup_handle`) were never affected because `s_fillStageResult` populates handles directly via `romProviderHandle(fileid)`, bypassing the catalog primary handle path. | `port/src/assetcatalog.c` | Build verify: queued `build-session.ps1 -Session passc-fix3 -Target client` PASS (CLIENT 23s, PerfectDark.exe 54.8 MB). Two ROM-relative pointer fields were never migrated when Pass C (`b15cc701`) freed `g_RomFile`: (1) `fileSlots[i].name` was set in `romdataInitFiles` as `(const char *)nameOffsets + ofs` where `nameOffsets = g_RomFile + ...`. After `sysMemFree(g_RomFile)`, every name pointer dangled. `assetCatalogRegisterBaseGame` -> `catalogBindPrimaryFromDiskOrRom` -> `romExtractRelPathForFilenum` -> `romdataFileGetName` returned a non-NULL but freed pointer; the next `romName[0]` deref AV'd. (2) `preprocessAnimations` set globals `_animationsTableRomStart` / `_animationsTableRomEnd` to `(segData + size - 0x38a0)` and `(segData + size)`. Both pointed into `g_RomFile` pre-Pass-C. After Pass C migrated the animations segment to a heap copy, the externs still pointed at the freed memory; `animsInit` (later in `pdmain.c::mainInit`) would `dmaExec` (memcpy) from the freed range and crash. Fix: in `romdataReleaseRom`, walk every fileSlot whose `.name` points into `[g_RomFile, g_RomFile + g_RomFileSize)` and `sysMemAlloc + memcpy` a heap-owned copy before freeing. Literal-string names (CDRCARROLL2 / CSKEDAR2 / GHAND_DRCARROLL / GHAND_SKEDAR set in `romdataInitFiles` as compile-time string literals in `.rdata`) stay as-is because they don't fall in the ROM range check. After migrating any segment, if the segment is named `"animations"` recompute the externs to point at the new heap buffer (same offset within `seg->size`). Audit confirmed no other preprocess publishes ROM-relative externs (`preprocessFont` / `preprocessALBankFile` already return heap; `preprocessSequences` / `preprocessJpnFont` / `preprocessTexturesList` / `preprocessMpConfigs` / `preprocessALCMidiHdr` only byteswap in place without setting externs). `ROMRELEASE` log line gains a `names migrated=N` count. Pre-existing logging artifact (modmgr `bodies=0 heads=0 arenas=0` cache rebuild, catalogInit `0 bodies, 1 heads`) is unrelated -- caused by `modmgrInit` running before `assetCatalogRegisterBaseGame` populates the catalog; modmgr's `modmgrEnsureCaches` / lazy fallback (`MODMGR_BASE_BODIES = 63`, `MODMGR_BASE_HEADS = 76`) lets `catalogInit` walk an empty cache that returns the zero-init slot 0, so only entry index 0 matches one head and the body lookup fails on `langGet`. The system self-corrects later when the catalog populates and `modmgrEnsureCaches` rebuilds; gameplay-time consumers see the right counts. Not Pass-C related; logged here for future cleanup. | `port/src/romdata.c` | Build verify: queued `build-session.ps1 -Session passc-fix -Target all/server/tests` ALL PASS (CLIENT 29s 54.8 MB, SERVER OK 22.4 MB, TESTS OK 24.9 MB). Targeted test pins green: `[catalog-mgr-body]` 16/190, `[catalog-mgr-arena]`, `[catalog-mgr-head]` 12/96, `[catalog-mgr-weapon]` 47, `[uichrome]` 7/56, `[passd]`, `[gate3]` 21/246. Pre-existing failure count unchanged (6 cases / 6 assertions) -- all in source files Pass C did not modify (`assetcatalog_base.c`, `loader_pdbase.h`, setup.c swarmBlock, cutscene_layer.c). Manual playtest: launch `Build/PerfectDark.exe`. Expect log `ROMRELEASE: g_RomFile released. segs migrated=N normalised=M skipped=K, fileSlots cleared=J, names migrated=L` with L > 0; expect title screen rendering (no AV before first frame). Run `Build/pd-client.log | grep -E "ROMRELEASE|FATAL|ACCESS_VIOLATION"`: no FATAL / AV between ROMRELEASE and stage transitions. |

Log evidence: `forgeTransitionToFreefly haschrbody=0 handmodeldef=NULL` AND `forgeTransitionToNormal haschrbody=0 handmodeldef=NULL` for the entire 3+ minute Grid session despite the body-swap-save log line firing at FREEFLY entry. Root cause: prior code only twiddled `chr->bodynum` / `chr->headnum` integer fields and trusted "the legacy model-reload pipeline picks up the change on the next per-chr model tick" -- empirically false. `chr->model` stayed at its allocation-time value (NULL when the player's chr was created on Grid stage entry because forge intercepted before TICKMODE_CUTSCENE / MPSWIRL could populate it). Bounded leak: the Dr Carroll model we allocate at FREEFLY entry is not explicitly freed -- per-transition slot release would require deeper modelmgr/gunmem plumbing; memory growth bounded to one alloc per FREEFLY entry, reclaimed at next stage unload. Visibility from camera: this fix makes the chr body MODEL load and the chr be marked as bodied; whether the freefly user SEES Dr Carroll in third-person depends on the camera mode at the time of rendering. The freefly camera is positioned at the chr (forgeApplyFreeflyToPlayer hijacks p->prop->pos to s_forge.fly.pos every tick), so the local user is effectively inside Dr Carroll. A camera-mode flip during freefly is a separate concern for the joint Menu Stacking + Input pillar session. | `src/game/forgemode.c` | Build verify: queued `build-session.ps1 -Session f5 -Target all` PASS (CLIENT 29s, PerfectDark.exe 54.5 MB). Verify by-eye: in third-person rendering paths (other player viewpoints, future co-op observers) the freefly user appears as Dr Carroll. (2) Add a LOUDFAIL channel `PREVIEW.FBO.BLACK:` in `pdgui_charpreview.c::pdguiCharPreviewRenderGBI` that fires when the FBO render path completes but `mm->bodymodeldef == NULL` (the silent-fail signal from menu.c). | **B-295** | HIGH | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-05-01 (S593g follow-up #5: integrated-head warning gate in body.c).** Mike's 2026-05-01 playtest log captured 550 `WARNING: CHR.DIAG: bodyAllocateModel head_canon=NULL for headnum=0` lines firing in waves at each swarm-cycle change (4 -> 8 -> 16 -> 32 -> 48 -> 64 -> 128 -> 256 = 556 bot allocations across the full ladder). Skedar (body 92) is an integrated-head body -- `catalogGetBodyIsComplete` returns true, the head geometry is part of the body model, the headnum slot is unused by the body alloc path. The warning at `body.c:405` was firing unconditionally for any `headnum >= 0 && head_canon == NULL` combination regardless of body type, so the swarm scenario filled the log with structurally-meaningless misses. Log file ended abruptly at 02:10.72 mid-spawn-flood -- 256 fopen/fwrite/fclose log calls in one frame (one warning per Skedar spawn) was a plausible contributor (file-flush stall under spawn pressure). **Fix**: extend the warning gate to `!catalogGetBodyIsComplete(bodynum)` in body.c. The warning still fires for separate-head bodies where a missing catalog head IS a real load-time problem; integrated-head bodies skip cleanly. **PRIOR S593f FIX still in place**: S593e's visual half-scale fix didn't touch the chr collision geometry (`chr->radius` / `chr->height`). Mike's playtest confirmed: "I think the tiny skedar still had regular sized colliders." With 256 chrs spawned at a single radius=600 ring, per-chr arc length was 14.7 vs 30-unit collision footprint -- every chr fully overlapped its neighbours, the per-chr push-resolution loop in `chrCalculatePushPos` could not find a clear push direction, and the bots froze in place exactly where they spawned (Mike's "256 wave didn't apply movement at all"). The crash that followed at 256 was likely a downstream consequence. **Fix**: (1) `chr->radius = 15` (half of 30) and `chr->height = 92` (half of chrInit default 185) at swarm spawn, matching the half visual scale; (2) multi-ring spawn distribution: SWARM_PER_RING=24 chrs per ring, ring step 200u, base 600u. At 256 chrs that's 11 rings reaching out to ~2600u with per-chr arc spacing always larger than the chr footprint. **PRIOR S593e FIX still in place**: S593d gave swarm bots hostile teams + aggressive AI + scale/health/speed + Debug Menu UX, but Mike's playtest found three remaining issues, two with a shared root cause. **Issue A (huge bots)**: `modelSetScale(chr->model, 0.5f)` directly REPLACED model->scale with 0.5, but `model->scale` is a multiplier on `model->definition->scale` which is ~1000 for chr bodies, and `bodyAllocateModel` initialises model->scale to a small value (~0.07 for Skedar via `scaleRaw * 0.1` in body.c:204 plus per-body height variation). Setting it to 0.5 produced ~7x natural size -> bots clipped into walls / ceilings on Car Park / Felicity. Fix: multiply by 0.5 (`chr->model->scale * 0.5f`) instead of replacing, preserving body-specific scaling. **Issue B (crash on 256 -> wrap to 4)**: playtest log showed repeated "WARNING: MODELMGR: All rwdata binding pools exhausted (type3=64) for rwdatalen=330 - heap fallback" starting at cycle 128. Skedar bodies have rwdatalen=330 words (Type 3 territory), NUMTYPE3 was 64. Over-cap chrs fell through to mempAlloc which is NOT freed by chrRemove -> heap-fallback chunks leak across cycles, exhausting MEMPOOL_STAGE after cycling 256 -> 4 -> 256 a few times -> next allocate crashes. Fix: bump NUMTYPE3 64 -> 320 in `modelmgr.c` + `modelmgrreset.c` (KEEP IN SYNC). Cost ~492 KB rwdata, acceptable. **Issue C (arena selector mismatch)**: playtest log showed `failed to resolve map_id='base:arena_mp_carpark'` with fallback to Felicity. The Debug Menu arena selector was passing `base:arena_*` (catalog ARENA id) but `testScenarioLaunch::resolve_map_stagenum` calls `catalogResolveStage` which matches `base:mp_*` (catalog STAGE id). Fix: at arena collect time, convert via `catalogStageIdByStagenum(arena_entry.stagenum)` so the selector stores stage ids that resolve. **PRIOR S593d FIX still in place**: S593c brought CPU bots up to real-bot ticks, but Mike's playtest found them "running aimlessly" -- the smoking gun was `chr->team = 1 << 7 = TEAM_NONCOMBAT (0x80)`. The engine's team taxonomy treats TEAM_NONCOMBAT as a "do not engage" signal, so bots had AI ticks but the hostility check classified them as non-combatants. Fix: dedicated swarm bot config (`s_SwarmBotConfig`: `BOTTYPE_KAZE` + `BOTDIFF_PERFECT`), `chr->team = TEAM_ENEMY` (different from player's TEAM_01), `aibot->command = AIBOTCMD_ATTACK` with `attackpropnum` = player. Half-scale via `modelSetScale(chr->model, 0.5f)`, half-health (`chr->maxdamage = 4.0`), 1.5x speed (PERFECT difficulty for CPU bots ~1.47x; max_speed 18 -> 27 for GPU compute path and gpu_fallback_seek_tick). Default arena changed from `base:mp_skedar` (cramped) to `base:mp_felicity` (open beach). Debug Menu UX redesigned: 3 radio modes (The Grid / CPU Bots / GPU Bots) + catalog-enumerated arena selector (`ASSET_ARENA`) + Start button. Team highlights suppressed naturally because `MPOPTION_TEAMSENABLED` is unset in our test scenario's `g_MpSetup.options`. GPU full-bot-AI remains the documented follow-up at [designs/in-flight/gpu-swarm-bot-pipeline.md](designs/in-flight/gpu-swarm-bot-pipeline.md). **PRIOR S593c FIX still in place**: First-pass S593 fix (2026-04-30) addressed the AV at PC `0x3ab39f` and a model-pool ceiling, but Mike's playtest revealed three remaining symptoms: cycler stuck at 8, Skedars moved as props with no real bot AI (CPU+GPU), no chr-vs-chr / chr-vs-player collision. **Cycler-stuck root cause**: `setup.c::chrmgrConfigure` was missing the same `testScenarioGetSwarmMaxCount()` swarm hook that `modelmgrAllocateSlots` already had. With `numchrs = PLAYERCOUNT() + 0 + 10` on a solo-no-simulants swarm session, `g_NumChrSlots = 11`, so the chr pool ran out after the player + ~10 chrs. Fix: mirror the swarm hook in the chrmgr path. **No-bot-AI / no-collision root cause**: swarm chrs were spawned with `ailist=GAILIST_IDLE` and no `aibot` -- they were ticking through `chraTick`'s passive paths only, no target acquisition, no movement intent, no collision-aware step. `chrSetPos` direct writes covered the visible-motion gap but bypassed the bot-ai-driven movement that handles chr-vs-chr collision via `chrTryStop`. Fix: CPU mode now spawns each chr as a real bot (`ailist=GAILIST_AIBOT_INIT`, `chr->myaction=MA_AIBOTMAINLOOP`, `botinvInit`), with `chr->aibot` pointing into a private 256-slot aibot pool that escapes the `MAX_BOTS=32` cap of `botmgrAllocateBot` while skipping its match-scoring registrations (`g_MpBotChrPtrs` etc.) that overflow at MAX_MPCHRS=40. CPU bots now seek/attack/dodge with collision. `chr->radius=30` is set so the perim has a meaningful size for the collision system. **GPU mode** keeps the position-only behaviour with `gpu_fallback_seek_tick` when compute is unavailable; full bot behaviour on GPU is filed as a follow-up pillar (`context/designs/in-flight/gpu-swarm-bot-pipeline.md`) since the gap is large (new compute kernel, AI state on GPU, GPU-side LOS/target acquisition). **First-pass fixes (S593, 2026-04-30) still in place**: `despawn_all` uses the canonical chrmgrStop pattern (`chrRemove + propDelist + propDisable + propFree`); `chrTick` early-outs with `TICKOP_FREE` + `CHR.STALE.MISS:` warning when `chr == NULL || chr->chrnum < 0 || chr->model == NULL`; NUMTYPE1/2/3 bumped 80/320/64 so 256 chrs fit in the rwdata binding pool; `apply_player_setup()` re-asserts invincibility / all-guns / bottomless ammo every tick; cycle ladder 4/8/16/32/48/64/128/256. | `port/src/swarm_test.c`; `port/fast3d/swarm_gpu.cpp`; `port/src/testscenarios.c`; `port/fast3d/pdgui_menu_mainmenu.cpp` (S593d, 2026-05-01); `src/game/setup.c`; `context/designs/in-flight/gpu-swarm-bot-pipeline.md` (S593c, 2026-05-01); `port/include/swarm_test.h`; `src/game/chr.c`; `src/game/modelmgr.c`; `src/game/modelmgrreset.c` (S593, 2026-04-30) | Manual playtest: Settings -> Debug -> Test Scenarios. Default mode = CPU Bots, default arena = Felicity. Click Start. Verify: cycler reaches 256 (4->8->16->32->48->64->128->256->4 via D-pad-Down or [0]); Skedars are half-size, chase + fire on the player aggressively, do not pass through each other or the player; switching mode to GPU Bots and a different arena via the selector launches that arena (GPU bots are position-only per `gpu-swarm-bot-pipeline.md`). Log invariants: `CHRSLOTS: added 256 swarm chr slots for benchmark` at level load; no `CHR.STALE.MISS:` warnings; no `0xc0000005` exception. |
| **B-294** | MED | **FIXED-PENDING-BUILD 2026-04-29 (S587).** The Public Mods publishing UI accepted free-form mod IDs/display metadata, and the request handler constructed local installed-mod paths from a peer-supplied ID. That let stale or typed IDs advertise as public mods and left a network-driven path-probing shape in the creator-content transfer surface. Fix: Public Mods publishing now selects an installed valid `modmgr` entry, public IDs are constrained by a safe-ID validator, broadcasts and requests skip stale/invalid entries, requests only serve explicitly published local mods, `.pdmod` archive paths come from `modmgr`, and saved registry strings are JSON-escaped. Static coverage pins the registry-backed UI, safe-ID gate, JSON escaping, and removal of the old path-probe pattern. | `port/src/social_share.c`; `port/fast3d/pdgui_friends.cpp`; `tests/test_public_mods_static.cpp` | Build in an isolated session with `.\devtools\build-session.ps1 -Session pm588 -Target tests -BuildTimeoutSeconds 600`, then run `.\.claude\session-builds\pm588\pd-tests.exe "[social][public_mods][static]"`. Manual public-mod smoke: enable an installed `.pdmod`, add it from Social -> Public Mods, confirm it broadcasts/downloads by ID, and confirm unsafe IDs / stale deleted mods do not broadcast or serve files. |
| **B-293** | MED | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-05-21 (c3828 follow-up).** Invite/group-session and live-spectator remote handoffs resolved an endpoint and then called raw `netStartClient(addr)`, bypassing the direct-connect -> hole-punch waterfall used by the normal join UI. That made some online entry paths less interoperable for NATed client-hosted sessions even though connect-code/menu joins already used `netStartClientWithHolePunch`. Fix: route `group_session.c::onPairOpen()` and `spectator.c::spectatorBeginLive()` through `netStartClientWithHolePunch()`, update comments/logs, and add static `[net][interoperability][static]` coverage for player-facing remote handoffs plus listen-host NAT startup/cleanup. c3828 verification confirmed both handoffs are hole-punch-aware and no raw `netStartClient(addr)` remains in those two handoff files. | `port/src/net/group_session.c`; `port/include/net/group_session.h`; `port/src/spectator.c`; `tests/test_net_lifecycle_static.cpp` | Verified: isolated `[c3828]` passed (includes `[net][interoperability][static][c3828]`) and queued isolated all-target build `c3828social` passed. Manual online test: host a listen game behind NAT, join via connect code, accept an invite/group-session handoff, and start live spectating from the same endpoint. Expected: all three paths log/use `netStartClientWithHolePunch`, attempt direct connect first, then punch/diagnose on timeout. |
| **B-292** | LOW | **FIXED 2026-05-20.** `devtools/run-pd-tests.ps1 -Session cat584 -Scope catalog-provider` failed before build under `Set-StrictMode` with `The property 'Count' cannot be found on this object.` Root cause was `$listModes = @($ListTags.IsPresent, $ListTests.IsPresent) \| Where-Object { $_ }`: with zero matching values, PowerShell collapsed the pipeline result to `$null`, so `$listModes.Count` threw before the targeted test helper could run. Fix: wrap the pipeline result in an outer array so `$listModes` is always countable. | `devtools/run-pd-tests.ps1` | Verified under B-355: `.\devtools\run-pd-tests.ps1 -Session pdtwrap -Selector "[modding][pdxxx][examples][static][c3811][c3812]" -BuildTimeoutSeconds 120` reached the build and passed 89 assertions in 1 case. |
| **B-291** | MED | **FIXED 2026-04-29 (S584).** Isolated build/test wrapper could misreport successful CMake configure as failure when the child process exit code came back blank and CMake only emitted warnings; CMake version probes used bare `git`, so PATH contamination could invoke devkitPro Git; and the first Ninja generated-header work could stall with no observable command/output. Fix: explicit per-step `.exit` files and missing-exit diagnostics; preferred/nonfatal `PD_GIT_EXECUTABLE` CMake probes; build prelude filters devkitPro MSYS Git; generated headers run as direct verbose `pd_headers`; target builds use direct verbose Ninja; per-step heartbeat logs record process trees/output sizes/.ninja_log; `build-headless.ps1 -SelfTest` locks the warning-exit-zero/nonzero-exit behavior. | `CMakeLists.txt`; `devtools/_build-env-prelude.ps1`; `devtools/build-headless.ps1`; `devtools/build-session.ps1` | Verified: parser checks, `build-headless -SelfTest`, queued `pipefix` tests build with 600s watchdog produced `pd-tests.exe`, `build-session -Tail -Session pipefix` surfaced logs, `[catalog][checked][regression]` passed 10/36, and `pipefix` cleanup was confirmed. |
| **B-290** | MED | **FIXED-PENDING-BUILD 2026-04-28 (S575).** `netmsgClcMoveRead()` parsed `outmoveack` and `netplayermove` but did not return on parse error before running state/weapon-select logic and committing `srccl->outmoveack`. A truncated or malformed `CLC_MOVE` could therefore let partially decoded move data influence validation or acknowledge server moves. Fix: return immediately after `netbufReadPlayerMove()` if `src->error` is set, before weapon inventory checks or move state writes. Static coverage pins the parse-error gate before state, weapon, and ack commits. | `port/src/net/netmsg.c`; `tests/test_netbuf.cpp` | Build in an isolated session and run `pd-tests`. | **B-289** | LOW | **FIXED-PENDING-BUILD 2026-04-28 (S575).** `CLC_ROOM_CREATE` silently treated unknown access-mode bytes as open rooms, and password-room requests with an empty password were downgraded to open. That weakened client-requested room access semantics and made corrupted room-create packets fail open. Fix: reject access modes above `ROOM_ACCESS_INVITE`, and reject password rooms with empty passwords before `roomCreateConfigured()`. Static coverage pins both checks before room creation and forbids the old downgrade text. | `port/src/net/netmsg.c`; `tests/test_net_lifecycle_static.cpp` | Build in an isolated session and run `pd-tests`. Negative room test: send room-create packets with access `> ROOM_ACCESS_INVITE` or password mode plus empty password; host should log rejection and not create a room. |
| **B-288** | LOW | **FIXED-PENDING-BUILD 2026-04-28 (S575).** Several room mutation handlers (`CLC_ROOM_CREATE`, `CLC_ROOM_JOIN`, `CLC_ROOM_LEAVE`, `CLC_ROOM_SETTINGS_UPDATE`, `CLC_ROOM_PLAYLIST_UPDATE`) decoded their payload and then dereferenced `srccl` for rate limits, room membership, or rebroadcast filtering without an explicit source-client guard. The live dispatcher normally supplies a client, but direct handler reuse or future dispatch changes could turn a malformed source into a crash in listen-host room UI paths. Fix: reject missing `srccl` before the first room-state access in each handler. Static coverage pins the guard ordering. | `port/src/net/netmsg.c`; `tests/test_net_lifecycle_static.cpp` | Build in an isolated session and run `pd-tests`. Negative handler test: call each room mutation reader with `srccl == NULL`; it should return failure before touching room state or sending rebroadcasts. |
| **B-287** | MED | **FIXED-PENDING-BUILD 2026-04-28 (S575).** `CLC_BOT_MOVE` trusted its record count before applying delegated bot-authority state. An over-counted packet from the authority client could mix valid early records with impossible trailing records, leaving host-side bot stubs partially updated before the stream failed later. Fix: reject counts above `MAX_BOTS` or above the current `g_BotCount` immediately after the count byte is parsed and before authorization-dependent state writes. Static coverage pins the count guard before `prop->syncid` and other bot state commits. | `port/src/net/netmsg.c`; `tests/test_net_lifecycle_static.cpp` | Build in an isolated session and run `pd-tests`. Negative packet test: delegated authority sends `CLC_BOT_MOVE` with count greater than active bots; host should log malformed count and leave bot stub state unchanged. |
| **B-286** | MED | **FIXED-PENDING-BUILD 2026-04-28 (S575).** `CLC_MANIFEST_STATUS` accepted any status byte and did not require the echoed `manifest_hash` to match the active server manifest before updating ready-gate state. A stale or forged response could mark a client ready/declined against the wrong manifest, or leave an unknown status silently ignored after parsing attacker-controlled missing IDs. Fix: reject statuses above `MANIFEST_STATUS_DECLINE`, and while the ready gate is active reject manifest-hash mismatches before parsing missing IDs or setting ready/decline bits. Static coverage pins both checks before ready-gate commits. | `port/src/net/netmsg.c`; `tests/test_net_lifecycle_static.cpp` | Build in an isolated session and run `pd-tests`. Negative packet test: send `CLC_MANIFEST_STATUS` with an invalid status or stale hash during match preparation; host should log rejection and not set ready/decline bits or queue asset transfer. |
| **B-285** | LOW | **FIXED-PENDING-BUILD 2026-04-28 (S575).** `CLC_AUTH` read the client-supplied local-player count byte but did not validate or use it. Today that made the field mostly inert, but it left a malformed-auth trust boundary where a later feature could accidentally rely on zero or out-of-range split-screen counts after auth had already accepted the client. Fix: reject `players == 0` or `players > MAX_PLAYERS` after payload-error checking and before ROM/mod checks or auth state commits. Static coverage pins the guard ordering. | `port/src/net/netmsg.c`; `tests/test_net_lifecycle_static.cpp` | Build in an isolated session and run `pd-tests`. A malformed response string could set netbuf error late and still leave a partially updated online row. Fix: parse into locals first, reject on any netbuf error, then commit all metadata and `online=true` together. | **B-283** | MED | **FIXED-PENDING-BUILD 2026-04-28 (S575).** Invalid internal addresses loaded from `pd.ini` could remain in `g_NetLastJoinAddr` / `g_NetRecentServers` until later UI/query paths encountered them. Fix: after `configInit()`, `netConfigSanitizeLoadedAddresses()` clears invalid `LastJoinAddr`, compacts invalid recent-server entries out of the list, and the server list shows an invalid-entry placeholder instead of raw text. | **B-277** | MED | **FIXED-PENDING-MANUAL-VERIFY 2026-04-28 (S572).** `netmsgSvcLobbyStateRead()` accepted any server-provided lobby mode/status bytes, committed `g_NetGameMode`, and treated any status `>= 2` as in-game. A malformed lobby-state packet from the server path could therefore leave local lobby/global mode state outside the declared mode/status domains. Fix: reject truncated input, any mode outside MP/Co-Op/Counter-Op, and any status outside 0..2 before committing `g_NetGameMode`, `g_Lobby.settings`, or `g_Lobby.inGame`. | **B-276** | HIGH | **FIXED-PENDING-MANUAL-VERIFY 2026-04-28 (S572).** `netmsgSvcStageStartRead()` accepted any server-provided mode byte and committed it to `g_NetGameMode` before branching into mission/combat setup writes. A malformed or hostile `SVC_STAGE_START` could therefore leave an out-of-range global mode even if later parsing failed. Fix: reject truncated mode reads and any mode outside MP/Co-Op/Counter-Op before committing `g_NetGameMode`. | **B-274** | HIGH | **FIXED-PENDING-MANUAL-VERIFY 2026-04-28 (S572).** `netmsgClcLobbyStartRead()` committed `g_NetGameMode = gamemode` and `g_NetCounterOpClientId = antiClientId` before validating the requested Counter-Op anti client. A rejected invalid, disconnected, or wrong-room anti client could therefore leave global mode/anti-client state mutated even though the start request returned failure. Fix: keep `counterOpClientId` local until invalid-id, lobby-ready, and room-membership rejection paths have all passed, then commit `g_NetGameMode` and `g_NetCounterOpClientId` together. Added a static lifecycle guard that pins all Counter-Op rejection returns before the global commits and forbids direct `g_NetCounterOpClientId = antiClientId`.
