The model-preload guard at `setup.c:2870-2872` only excluded the LEGACY sentinels (`!= 0xFF && != 0`); 0xFE flowed through and `modelmgrLoadProjectileModeldefs(254)` indexed `g_Weapons[254]` -- a 254-byte walk past the end of the [WEAPON_SUICIDEPILL + 1] = 86-entry array (`src/include/game/inv.h:9`), reading garbage adjacent .data and AVing on `weapon->functions[i]`. The original FIESTA design comment ("chosen as 0xFE so any code path checking `!= 0xFF && != 0` continues to exclude this value as well") was mathematically wrong: `0xFE != 0xFF AND 0xFE != 0` both hold. Same incomplete-exclusion pattern was latent in the userPickedSpawnWeapon predicate at setup.c:2775, the bot.c:543 elif, and the player.c:1835 elif (the latter two were structurally safe because the FIESTA-mode branch above caught 0xFE first, but the predicate text drifted). **Bad-value class:** READ-WRONG / sentinel-not-handled. **Fix (4 changes per Mike's no-half-measures + INV-1 loud-fail directive):** (1) shared single-source-of-truth predicate `spawnWeaponNumIsResolved(num)` static inline in `port/include/net/matchsetup.h` returning 0 for {0, 0xFF, SPAWNWEAPON_FIESTA_SENTINEL}; (2) replaced four open-coded sentinel checks with the helper at setup.c:2781, setup.c:2878, bot.c:543, player.c:1835; (3) defensive bound + INV-1 loud-fail (`WEAPON.SLOT.MISS:`) inside `modelmgrLoadProjectileModeldefs` -- weaponnum out of [0, ARRAYCOUNT(g_Weapons)) returns false with LOG_WARNING instead of AVing, defence in depth against any future caller that bypasses the upstream gate; (4) pd-tests case `tests/test_spawn_weapon_resolved.cpp` -- 10 cases / 860 assertions covering the helper contract (including exhaustive 0..0xFF walk that catches future sentinel-addition drift) plus the consumer-gate-denies-FIESTA invariant Mike named. **Methodology learning** (captured in commit message + constraint): when adding a new sentinel value, audit EVERY consumer of the field, not just the writer that introduced the sentinel. The FIESTA sentinel was added cleanly to matchStart's writer (S482) but downstream consumers kept the legacy two-value exclusion that the new sentinel was not designed to satisfy. Build clean (PerfectDark.exe + PerfectDarkServer.exe + pd-tests). [b263] tag: 10 cases / 860 assertions pass. | `port/include/net/matchsetup.h` (helper); `src/game/setup.c::setupCreateProps` (two predicate sites); `src/game/modelmgrreset.c::modelmgrLoadProjectileModeldefs` (leaf bound); `src/game/bot.c` + `src/game/player.c` (sentinel-check migration); `tests/test_spawn_weapon_resolved.cpp` (new). | **Playtest:** start a Combat Sim match with spawn weapon mode = Fiesta. Subsequent player + bot spawns must roll fresh weapons per spawn (visible in HUD weapon names changing per respawn). | **B-261** | CRITICAL | **MITIGATED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-04-27 (S483b, this session).** ACCESS_VIOLATION 0xc0000005 at PC RVA 0x396ed2 mid-mission on Investigation (stage 0x33), LVTICK frame 1836, ~30s after spawn. addr2line places the fault at `src/lib/model.c:1387` (`sp2c.z = rodata->reorder.unk08;`) inside `modelUpdateReorderRelations`. Disassembly shows reads at offsets 0x28 / 0xc / 0x10 / 0x14 / 0x0 / 0x4 succeeded; the +0x8 read AVs -- consistent with the rodata struct straddling a page boundary into unmapped memory. Same frame logged `WARNING: DOOR.DIAG: doorGetBbox -- no bbox for modelnum=154 model=0x0000019939568438 flags=0x80 doortype=0 (count=1)` (the door's `model_009a` had a missing/unreadable bbox node that the existing defensive guard caught and logged); the REORDER traversal had no equivalent guard and the AV crashed the process. Mike's hypothesis (sharpening the post-mortem candidate list): the catalog or model-load pipeline is registering the door entry without finishing its rodata pool, leaving some nodes with unreadable rodata that succeed at low-byte reads but fail at the page boundary. Mitigation this session ships: (a) per-tick rodata-validity guard via VirtualQuery probe in `modelUpdateReorderRelations`, `modelUpdateDistanceRelations`, `modelApplyDistanceRelations`, `modelApplyToggleRelations`, `modelApplyReorderRelationsByArg` -- on miss logs `MODEL.RODATA.MISS: site=<X> model=<P> node=<P> rodata=<P> bytes=<N> reason=<NULL\|page-unmapped> (count=<n>)` rate-limited per call site and skips the node safely; (b) load-time tree-walk validator in `setupLoadModeldef` (after `modeldefLoadToNew` succeeds) probes every node's rodata once and logs `MODEL.RODATA.LOAD: PARTIAL modelnum=<N> fileid=<X> id=<id> nodes=<T> bad=<B> reorder=<R> distance=<D> toggle=<T> bbox=<B> dl=<L>` so we can identify partial loads at load time before the tick path ever touches them. The mitigation prevents the AV but does NOT fix the upstream load incompleteness -- post-playtest forensic logs (`MODEL.RODATA.LOAD: PARTIAL` lines) discriminate between Mike's catalog-data-missing hypothesis and the alternate use-after-free path; root cause then lands on the catalog/load side. **Cohort context:** mirrors INV-1 (b6a0c280) loud-fail discipline -- the diagnostic channel name `MODEL.RODATA.MISS:` parallels `CATALOG.MISS:`. New file `port/src/model_rodata_guard.c` houses the VirtualQuery probe + per-site rate limiter; `port/include/model_rodata_guard.h` exposes the int-returning predicates (no `<stdbool.h>` to keep the header includable from `src/lib/model.c` where `bool` is the `s32` macro). | `port/src/model_rodata_guard.c` + `port/include/model_rodata_guard.h` (new); `src/lib/model.c::modelUpdateReorderRelations / modelUpdateDistanceRelations / modelApplyDistanceRelations / modelApplyToggleRelations / modelApplyReorderRelationsByArg` (per-tick guards); `src/game/setuputils.c::setupLoadModeldef + setupValidateModeldefRodata` (load-time tree walk). | **Playtest:** repro Mike's path -- start Investigation mission, run for >= 30s of normal gameplay. Crash must NOT recur. Search `pd-client.log` for `MODEL.RODATA.LOAD: PARTIAL` lines after the manifest load -- if any model logs PARTIAL with bad>0, that confirms the load pipeline is producing incomplete rodata for that modelnum (Mike's catalog-data-missing hypothesis). Search for `MODEL.RODATA.MISS:` lines during gameplay -- if these fire it means a node escaped load-time detection and the per-tick guard is the only thing preventing the AV. Forward both sets of logs for catalog-side root-cause discrimination. |

| **B-253b** | HIGH | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-04-25 (B-253 follow-up: framing + missing-render + Shift+F1/F2 diagnostic toggles + Grid auto-wireframe).** Mike's playtest of `21010fbd` showed three new symptoms: (1) "really zoomed in or just a 2D texture" — camera framing at face-fills-frame zoom. (2) "for many characters it doesn't appear at all" — body-dependent invisible rendering. (3) Mike requested a backface-cull toggle to diagnose normals-flipped bodies. Root cause for (1): `menuRenderModel`'s chr-skel branch dynamically rewrites `menumodel->zoom` from a 480-tick triangle-wave oscillator (range 100..370) keyed on `zoomtimer60`, ignoring the value the caller wrote. We never set `zoomtimer60`, so it sat at 0 → frac=0 → `zoom = 100 + (1-0)*270 = 370` (CLOSE end). Fix: `pdguiCharPreviewRenderGBI` now sets `mm->zoomtimer60 = TICKS(240)` before each `menuRenderModel` so the oscillator pins to its hold-at-1 window → zoom=100 (the legacy-tuned FAR end). Root cause for (2): `menuRenderModel` declares 7 local floats (`rotx/roty/rotz/posx/posy/posz/scale`) without init at function entry. The HUDPIECE branch sets them from `menumodel->cur*`; the DEFAULT branch (used for character preview) reads them uninitialized — pure UB. Stack contents that happened to be 0 → `mtx00015f04(scale*zoomy)` = 0 → invisible. Stack contents that happened to be huge → model overflowed FBO. Variability across bodies tracked which other code paths ran in between. Fix: initialize all seven to safe defaults (0 for pos/rot, 1.0 for scale) at function entry. HUDPIECE branch unaffected (overwrites). DEFAULT case now deterministic. Also added `g_MenuPreviewBypassChrZoom` (extern bool) for future tuning if zoom=100 is still too zoomed for some bodies — bypass routes through bbox path with caller-supplied zoom. For (3): two diagnostic toggles for the WORLD render path (per Mike's correction "applies to main world render, not character preview"). `Shift+F1` cycles cull mode `none → back → front → none`. `Shift+F2` toggles wireframe overlay (`glPolygonMode GL_LINE`). State + per-draw apply in `port/fast3d/gfx_opengl.cpp::gfx_opengl_apply_debug_state`; gated on `current_framebuffer == 0` so toggles never bleed into the character-preview FBO render. Defaults preserve PD's legacy "cull-none, fill" so no rendering regression. Key handlers + combined on-screen amber-pill indicator in `pdgui_backend.cpp`. **Grid auto-wireframe**: `forgeTransitionToNormal` and `forgeTransitionToFreefly` save the user's current cull/wireframe state and auto-enable wireframe; `forgeTransitionToInactive` restores. Mike's "in The Grid the freefly camera flies through walls" use case — wireframe lets him see geometry edges of distant rooms. User can override mid-Grid via Shift+F1/F2; restore is to the pre-Grid user state, not the auto-set state. Save/restore is idempotent so NORMAL ↔ FREEFLY hops don't clobber the saved state. **Layout** per Mike's "fill remaining space" feedback: left content-sized (`pdguiScale(320)` Agent Creator / `pdguiScale(360)` Character Select, capped at 50% width); right pane fills remaining rectangle (no longer forced square). Aspect-aware projection: `pdguiCharPreviewSetAspect()` exposed; `pdgui_model_preview` computes `aspect = pane_w / pane_h` and passes through; `pdguiCharPreviewRenderGBI` plumbs into `viSetFovAspectAndSize`. Square 512×512 FBO + matching projection aspect cancel out into the displayed rect — character proportions stay correct. | `src/game/menu.c::menuRenderModel` (init + bypass flag); `src/include/game/menu.h` (extern); `port/fast3d/pdgui_charpreview.c` (zoomtimer60 pin + aspect setter); `port/include/pdgui_charpreview.h` (aspect API); `port/fast3d/pdgui_model_preview.cpp` (compute aspect from pane); `port/fast3d/pdgui_menu_agentcreate.cpp` (content-sized left + fill-remaining right); `port/fast3d/pdgui_menu_playerconfig.cpp` (same); `port/fast3d/gfx_opengl.cpp` (cull + wireframe state + per-draw apply gated on current_framebuffer); `port/fast3d/pdgui_backend.cpp` (Shift+F1/F2 + combined indicator); `src/game/forgemode.c` (Grid auto-wireframe save/restore via forge transitions). | **Playtest matrix:** (1) Agent Creator — left ~320px controls, right pane fills remaining width AND height. Character renders at full-body framing, centered, 3/4-turn pose. (2) Cycle through ALL bodies — every one renders. None invisible. None gigantic. None zoomed-to-face. (3) Character Select — same expectations, left ~360px scrollable body list. (4) `Shift+F1` once: top-right amber pill "Cull Back". Press again: "Cull Front" (some/all geometry should disappear). Press third: indicator gone. (5) Diagnostic: any body still missing → toggle `Shift+F1` to mode 2; if it appears, that body's normals are inverted. (6) Lobby portrait + Skin Editor regression checks pass. (7) Resize window: layout adapts, character aspect stays correct. |
| **B-259** | HIGH | **STRUCTURALLY-RESOLVED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-04-25 (this session, follow-up to K-c).** Mike playtested build `21010fbd` and reported "Mouse is usable but not visible." That is exactly the K-c MEDIUM drift the post-implementation audit flagged: the "inputCtxSyncMouseMode is the SOLE SDL_ShowCursor authority" claim in the K-c commit was overstated. Four writers existed: `inputctx.c::inputCtxSyncMouseMode` (ctx push/pop), `input.c::inputMouseShowCursor` (MLOCK_AUTO 3-second auto-hide timer), `gfx_sdl2.cpp::gfx_sdl_set_cursor_visibility` (gfx-API surface), `pdgui_backend.cpp` (B-92 hotswap-close deferred-mouse-capture flush). Bad-value triage: the writers fired under different conditions; in steady gameplay both authorities pointed at "hide" via different paths and agreed; in steady menu state inputctx said "show" but the MLOCK_AUTO timer in `inputUpdateMouse()` (input.c:765) silently fired `SDL_ShowCursor(SDL_DISABLE)` every tick once the user had been idle 3 seconds (CURSOR_HIDE_TIME), since `mouseLockMode == MLOCK_AUTO && !mouseLocked` was true (mouseLocked = false in menus). The menu still consumed click events because SDL delivers them regardless of cursor visibility. inputCtxSyncMouseMode's "set if not yet set" guard `if (SDL_GetRelativeMouseMode())` couldn't recover -- relative mode was already SDL_FALSE in menus, so the menu branch never re-fired SDL_ShowCursor(ENABLE). **Fix:** collapse to one authority via new public helper `inputCtxApplyCursorVisibility(s32 want_show)` in `port/src/inputctx.c` + `port/include/inputctx.h`. The helper checks `inputCtxGetTop() != &g_CtxGameplay`; if true, the caller's hide request is suppressed and the cursor is force-shown. All four pre-existing writers now route through the helper: `inputMouseShowCursor` (gates the auto-hide timer), `gfx_sdl_set_cursor_visibility` (gfx-API), pdgui_backend B-92 hotswap close. inputCtxSyncMouseMode itself now re-asserts cursor visibility via the helper unconditionally on every ctx push/pop event (no more "set if not yet set" guard for visibility), so even if a future writer drifts the SDL flag mid-session, the next ctx event re-syncs. The K-c `ImGuiConfigFlags_NoMouseCursorChange` fix still neutralises the ImGui SDL backend's independent SDL_ShowCursor calls. Build clean (PerfectDark.exe 55 MB). Runtime smoketest 10s confirms ctx push/pop -> syncMouseMode firing as expected; the MLOCK_AUTO timer silently re-asserts every tick but the helper observes ctx top != gameplay and suppresses the hide. | `port/include/inputctx.h` (new public helper declaration); `port/src/inputctx.c::inputCtxApplyCursorVisibility` + `inputCtxSyncMouseMode` (now uses helper); `port/src/input.c::inputMouseShowCursor` (routes through helper); `port/fast3d/gfx_sdl2.cpp::gfx_sdl_set_cursor_visibility` (routes through helper); `port/fast3d/pdgui_backend.cpp` (hotswap-close path routes through helper + comment block updated to reflect the four-writer collapse). | **Playtest:** (1) Open any menu (Settings, pause, Combat Sim setup). Leave mouse idle for 5+ seconds (longer than 3s CURSOR_HIDE_TIME). Cursor must remain visible. (2) Move mouse, click any UI element. Click registers AND cursor was visible at the time of the click. (3) Resume gameplay. Cursor hides immediately (gameplay context honours the auto-hide). (4) Re-pause: cursor instantly visible. (5) Repeat 3x to stress transitions. (6) See `context/audits/input-authority-discipline-2026-04-25.md` section G + `context/designs/input-authority-methodology.md`. |

The Theme editor's "Save as .pdmod" should produce a file at `mods/<slug>.pdmod` and the new theme should appear in the selector after `pdguiThemeRescanMods`. |

| **B-253** | HIGH | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-04-25 (Priority Q -- 3D render box).** Mike: "we still don't have a working render box for 3d stuff. Such as in the Agent creator". Mike's logs showed `pdguiCharPreviewRequest` firing but the visible preview didn't appear in Agent Creator (Character Select had the same problem when the preview was the smaller 300x340 panel; lobby portraits in S352 happened to work because the room-dialog screen rect overlapped (0..256, 0..256) of the FBO -- accidental, not by design). **Root cause** (system-level, not line-level): `menuRenderModel`'s MENUMODELTYPE_DEFAULT path calls `func0f0d49c8` which emits `gSPViewport(viGetCurrentPlayerViewport())` -- the player's full-screen viewport, NOT the FBO's small viewport. It also calls `menuApplyScissor(gdl)` which emits `gDPSetScissor` from `g_MenuScissorX1/X2/Y1/Y2` -- the screen-relative dialog rect, NOT the FBO bounds. So with the FBO bound but the viewport + scissor in screen-coordinates, the model lands at the screen pixel coords of whatever menu rect was last computed. The FBO captures only the intersection of those rects with (0,0)-(W,H). For Agent Creator the dialog rect was outside (0..256,0..256), so the FBO captured nothing -> black image -> `pdgui_model_preview` fell back to the silhouette placeholder. **Fix** (`port/fast3d/pdgui_charpreview.c::pdguiCharPreviewRenderGBI`): before calling `menuRenderModel`, save the global view state (`g_MenuScissor*`, `g_Vars.currentplayer->viewport[g_ViBackIndex]`, vi back-data view dims + fov + aspect) and override to FBO-local values: scissor (0,0,W,H), player viewport overwritten in-place to FBO viewport, view dims = FBO size, aspect = 1.0. Render into the FBO. Restore CPU state, then emit GBI restoration commands using the restored values. The FBO now contains a properly-rendered character; `ImGui::Image` samples the texture; the live preview reaches the menu. **Also bumped FBO from 256x256 to 512x512** so a 1/2-screen render box stays sharp; viewport math is parameterized so this is a one-line change. **Also locked the render path to `g_Menus[0].menumodel`** so request submit and render reader always agree on which menu struct holds the pending state (PC has MAX_LOCAL_PLAYERS=1, so g_MpPlayerNum=0 always). **Layout work:** Agent Creator (`pdgui_menu_agentcreate.cpp`) and MP Character Select (`pdgui_menu_playerconfig.cpp::renderMpCharacter`) both reorganized per Mike's brief: LEFT = control rows (Name + Body carousel + Head carousel for Agent Creator; Body list + Head carousel for Character Select), RIGHT = large square 3D render pane. 3D pane uses fixed 3/4-turn pose (rotY = -0.45 rad), idle rotation off. Catalog-source-of-truth respected at every layer: catalog ID strings flow from menu selections through `pdguiCharPreviewRequest` -> `assetCatalogResolve` -> catalog `mp_index` -> `catalogGetBodyFilenumByIndex` (last-mile to legacy `modeldefLoad`). | `port/fast3d/pdgui_charpreview.c` (FBO viewport fix + 256->512 bump + g_Menus[0] lock); `port/fast3d/pdgui_menu_agentcreate.cpp` (left-controls + right-3D-pane); `port/fast3d/pdgui_menu_playerconfig.cpp::renderMpCharacter` (flipped layout + larger pane); `port/fast3d/pdgui_model_preview.cpp` (unchanged -- consumer-side already correct). | **Playtest matrix:** (1) **Agent Creator** -- Main Menu -> Choose Agent -> New Agent. Window opens with "Create Agent" title, LEFT pane shows Agent Name input, Character carousel, Head carousel; RIGHT pane shows a square 3D render box. The render box must show the actual character model (head + body), not a silhouette placeholder. Cycle Body via < / > arrows: render updates immediately. Cycle Head: render updates. (2) **MP Character Select** -- Main Menu -> Combat Sim -> Player Setup -> Character. LEFT pane shows Body list (scrollable) + Head carousel; RIGHT pane shows a square 3D render box. Click a body in the list: render updates. Cycle head with arrows: render updates. (3) **Lobby portraits regression check** (S352) -- enter a network room with multiple humans, confirm the per-slot portrait thumbnails still appear correctly (this fix hardens the render path; S352 baking still uses the same FBO). (4) **Skin Editor regression check** (Batch S-2/S-9) -- open Modding Hub -> Skin Editor on any character: the live preview pane still renders, skin overrides still apply, S-9 capture still works. (5) Confirm no regression in other charpreview-using screens: weapon/vehicle/prop training holograms, modding hub character preview. |

| **B-250** | MED | **STRUCTURALLY-RESOLVED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-04-25 (Priority K-b1 + K-b3).** Issue 2 in Mike's playtest framing: backing out of a Combat Sim flow leaves the menu in a "stuck" state -- the visual menu closes but input authority doesn't follow, or vice versa. K-b1 fix: `menupoolReleaseAll` now also pops the unregistered-fallback ctx if one is recorded, so callers can rely on a single bulk-release. K-b3 fix: deleted the paired `inputCtxPopDeferred(&g_CtxImGuiMenu)` calls from 8 force-close sites (3 in pdgui_bridge.c, 1 in net.c, 2 in matchsetup.c, 2 in netmsg.c). | **B-240** | LOW | **EXTENDED 2026-04-25 (Priority N).** Mike's earlier observation: bot tick load-spreading needed for full-bot matches to smooth frame time. Existing implementation: <=8 bots = no spread (60 Hz per bot), 9-16 = 2 groups (30 Hz), 17-24 = 3 groups (20 Hz). | **B-256** | MED | **RESOLVED-STRUCTURAL-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-04-25 (v43 wire field, commits 7fbc5833 + a91caf07 + this batch).** Two layers now: (1) **Wire-authoritative** -- `SVC_PLAYER_STATS` (v43) carries an `s8 attacker_id` populated by the producing peer via `mpPlayerGetIndex(chr->lastattacker)` at write time. Mixed v42/v43 play is rejected at the ENet auth handshake (`port/src/net/net.c:1560`) and at the presence proto check (`group_session.c:212`). (2) **Local fallback** -- the v42 third-site closure (commit a91caf07) at `netmsg.c:2128` remains as the fallback path when the wire reports -1 sentinel or self, plus the original 7fbc5833 fixes in chr.c and player.c for the local-sim death paths (bot pit-fall + symmetric playerDie). Both layers exist by design: wire-authoritative for the normal kill path, local-fallback for suicides / world damage / NULL attacker / pre-v43 grace edge cases. Kill-attribution path was reading `chr->lastshooter` (s16) and `chr->timeshooter`, but these legacy fields are never wired up in current code -- the only writes are `lastshooter = -1` resets. The live attribution data is in `chr->lastattacker` (struct chrdata pointer), set by chraction.c on damage. Fix: in chr.c (bot pit-fall death attribution) and player.c (playerDie symmetric path), read `chr->lastattacker` and resolve via `mpPlayerGetIndex(chr->lastattacker)` instead of the dead `lastshooter`/`timeshooter` pair. | **B-247** | HIGH | **FIXED v2 PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-04-25 (commit 4252d7a3 -- root cause).** v1 (2026-04-24) was a defensive read-side guard in `forgeSnapFreeflyToPlayer`. v2 lands the actual upstream fix: `cdFindGroundFromList` (collision.c:1819) initializes its accumulator to -2^32 (-4294967296) as a 'no ground found' sentinel; when no ground geo intersects the spawn-pad search cylinder, the sentinel was returned to `playerStartNewLife` / `mpOrchestrateMatchStartSpawns` which both did `pos.y = groundy + vv_eyeheight` -- giving ~-4.29e9, which propagated to prop->pos. The bug-log Y of -4294967040 = -4294967296 + 256 exactly matches this pattern. v2 fix: validate groundy at both spawn sites (|groundy| <= 100000); on sentinel, log WARNING and fall back to using the spawn pad's authored Y as the ground reference. Original v1 framing: Mike's Grid log `019dc2f6-pdclient.log` showed `GRID: -> FREEFLY (session start (request)) pos=(0,-4294967040,0) yaw=0.0`. The Y coordinate `-4294967040` is `0xFFFFFF00` interpreted as signed -- a clear uninitialized-memory signature, NOT a normal "spawned in the air with gravity" value. Root cause: `forgeSnapFreeflyToPlayer` (`src/game/forgemode.c:126`) blindly copied `p->prop->pos` into `s_forge.fly.pos` without sanity-checking. When stage 0x3e (STAGE_TEST_MP6 / Caves) is launched via the Grid menu's incorrectly-labelled "All Missions" entry (B-243), the player chr's pos may carry junk coords from an uninitialized or partially-configured spawn pipeline. The corrupt Y propagated through the rest of the freefly pipeline, the player fell through the level, hit the kill plane, and "Mission Failed" triggered (B-244 / B-245 cascade). **Fix:** at the top of `forgeSnapFreeflyToPlayer`, validate that `p->prop->pos` X / Y / Z are all within +/-100000 game units (sane stage coords are O(thousands)). If any axis is out of bound, log `GRID: forgeSnapFreeflyToPlayer: invalid player pos=(...) -- using fallback (0,100,0)` and use a deterministic fallback `(0, 100, 0)` so the observer at least spawns at world origin above ground rather than at the corrupt bit-pattern coord. Defensive only -- does not fix the upstream "why is the player chr at junk coords" issue (B-243 + the test_mp6 spawn pipeline), but it does prevent the cascade and gives Mike a recoverable state instead of falling-through-the-floor + abort-non-functional. | `src/game/forgemode.c::forgeSnapFreeflyToPlayer` | **Playtest:** (1) Select a normal Grid map, enter session: log shows `pos=(X,Y,Z)` with reasonable coordinates and no fallback warning. (2) Select the "All Missions" entry (still buggy until B-243's actual root cause lands): if the player chr has junk coords, the WARN line fires and the observer spawns at (0,100,0). | **B-246** | HIGH | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST round-2 2026-04-25 (Issue 1 -- visibility gate refactor).** Round-1 fix (`0dc0173d`, dropped `count >= 3` from `bgunCanFreeWeapon`) unblocked the deferred-switch consumer but Mike's playtest of `0dc0173d` (log `019dc344`) showed the FP weapon still invisible. Round-3 visibility-gate instrumentation pinned the new failure precisely: `gates: noFlag40=0 flag80=0 mode6=1 mode7=0 notLoaded=0 notInuse=0 memType0=0 / raw: hand_mode=6 gunmemowner=0 gunmemtype=1 gunmemnew=-1 masterload=4`. Every gate passed except `mode6=1` -- the `hand->mode == HANDMODE_6` check forced `visible=false` even though the master load was complete (`gunmemowner=BONDGUN`, `gunmemtype=UNARMED`, `gunmemnew=-1`, `masterloadstate=LOADED`) and `inuse=1`. `HANDMODE_6` is set during the LOWER -> LOAD transition (`bondgun.c:2967`) and only changes to `HANDMODE_7` when `bgun0f09bf44` returns true. Static analysis says all five `bgun0f09bf44` conditions should pass for Mike's data, yet runtime evidence proved the mode never transitioned -- something elusive is preventing the LOAD/HANDMODE_6 -> HANDMODE_7 hop. Round-2 fix: refactor the visibility gate so the mode=6/7 check is scoped to `state == CHANGEGUN && stateminor in (LOWER, RAISE)` -- the two stateminors where the gun is genuinely off-screen / mid-transition. LOAD and EQUIP are "settled" stateminors where the weapon model is on-screen and should render. The other gates (`bgunIsLoaded`, `inuse`, `gunmemtype != 0`, weapon-flag) still guarantee the weapon model is actually loaded. Bot path unaffected (separate third-person attach pipeline). Round-1 + round-2 together cover the bug end-to-end: round-1 ensures the deferred switch fires, round-2 ensures the FP weapon renders even if the post-switch state-machine transitions are stuck. **Original three-round instrumentation rationale:** Three rounds of runtime instrumentation pinned the failure: Mike's SP log (`019dc325`, build `7bcf083b`, Mission 1 direct entry) showed `bgunTickSwitch2 player=0 frame=38 switchto=1 R(canFree=0 state=5 sm=2 cnt=0 throwing=0)` -- the hand state machine reached CHANGEGUN/LOAD (state=5, stateminor=2) cleanly with no pending throw, but `bgunCanFreeWeapon` returned false for both hands because the gate's `count >= 3` requirement was unmet. Static analysis says `hand->count` should increment per frame in `bgunTickInc` while `lvupdate240 > 0`, and the count-reset sites (`bondgun.c:2865/2901/2971/3163`) are confined to UNEQUIP / LOWER stateminor and stateminor-advance transitions -- none should fire while sitting in LOAD/HANDMODE_6 waiting on master load. Despite that, the runtime log proved count was not advancing past 0/1 in this codepath. Without a fourth instrumented round to pin the elusive count reset, the safest structural fix is to drop the `count >= 3` guard entirely: the design intent was "wait a few frames after entering LOAD before swapping the weapon record", but the LOWER stateminor's existing `stateframes >= TICKS(16)` gate (`bondgun.c:2924`) already throttles -- LOAD only begins after the lower animation has run for ~16 ticks, so the hand is already settled by the time bgunCanFreeWeapon evaluates. Fix: change the gate from `state==CHANGEGUN && stateminor==LOAD && count>=3 && !throwing` to `state==CHANGEGUN && stateminor==LOAD && !throwing`. The dual-hand requirement (caller checks both R and L canFree) still ensures both hands reach LOAD before the swap commits. Unblocks the full chain: bgunTickSwitch2 fires -> weaponnum and gunmemnew set -> bgunTickMasterLoad acquires BONDGUN ownership and loads the FP model -> bgunIsLoaded() returns true -> hand->mode advances 6 -> 7 -> EQUIP -> stateminor advances LOAD -> RAISE -> EQUIP -> hand->visible flips true -> FP weapon renders, fire fires, pickup works. **Bug spans SP and MP because both share this exact gate** (per Mike's confirmation that the symptom repros in both). Three rounds of `LOG.WPN.DIAG:` instrumentation are kept in place as cheap, low-volume monitoring that will surface any regression. | `src/game/bondgun.c::bgunCanFreeWeapon` (drop the `count >= 3` check); instrumentation in `bondgun.c` / `player.c` / `prop.c` retained. | **Playtest matrix:** (1) SP Mission 1 (or any campaign mission), spawn: FP weapon (Falcon 2 / Falcon Silencer / whatever the mission grants) is visible immediately, fire fires, pickup works. (2) MP CS solo (Combat Simulator with 0 bots): same -- FP weapon visible, fire works, can pick up world weapons. (3) MP CS with bots: Mike's FP weapon works AND bots' weapons continue to work normally (the bot path was never broken, regression check). (4) Weapon-cycle mid-match: switch to a different weapon via radial / weapon next; new FP weapon loads and becomes visible. (5) The Grid -> Playtest match: spawn FP weapon visible. (6) Late-join MP: after match start, weapon visible immediately. (7) `LOG.WPN.DIAG:` lines should now show `bgunTickSwitch2` firing successfully on the first frame the hand reaches LOAD stateminor; `bgunRender` shows `R(visible=1)`; `fire handler enter` shows real `triggeron=1` events when Mike presses fire. |

| **B-242** | MED | **OPEN -- Priority O queued 2026-04-24.** Mike: "we will need to work on our spawning system, as I have spawned inside a wall a few times. I think it would help once picking a spawn point to sweep the test capsule in a radius to ensure it isn't clipping, and if so, place it into a position where it isn't." After picking a spawn point (player or bot), perform a capsule sweep at the chosen position; if the player capsule reports clipping (overlap with static geometry), iterate a radial sweep outward (small radius increments rotating around the spawn point) testing capsule overlap at each candidate until a non-clipping position is found OR a max-radius / max-iterations bound is hit. If the bound is hit without finding a clean position, fall back to the next spawn point in the rotation rather than spawning into geometry. Apply uniformly to player and bot spawn paths -- bots clipping into walls would manifest as confused / stuck-in-wall AI behaviour. The `spawn-raycast-budget` system already validates candidates via multi-ray cast against backfaces and a distance-sum budget threshold; the capsule sweep is the next layer up (a backface cast can say "ground is here" but a tight wall corner can still produce a capsule overlap). Build on top of the existing validation, don't replace it. Queued behind the B-243 / Issue 1 weapon investigation. After that resolves, slots in alongside Priority K (input-authority discipline) and Priority L (flat menu navigation). | Future implementation site: `src/lib/capsule.c` for the sweep helper, `src/game/spawnpool.c::spawnPoolSelectTiered` for the player path, `src/game/bot.c::botSpawn` plus the orchestrator in `src/game/mplayer/mpspawn_orchestrate.c` for bot paths. | **Future verification:** spawn 16 bots on tight-corridor stages (Felicity, Skedar arenas) and confirm none manifest stuck-in-wall behaviour. Run capsule overlap check after every player respawn for one full match without false-positive false-clear (verify by deliberately re-using a clipping spawn pad and confirming the radial sweep finds a clean position within the bound). Log `SPAWN.CLIP: bot=N original=(x,z) sweep_iters=M placed=(x',z')` on each rescue, `SPAWN.CLIP: bot=N original=(x,z) sweep_failed -- falling back to next pad` on bound-exceeded fallback. |
| **B-239** | LOW | **DEFENSIVE HARDENING 2026-04-24.** Frame: this is a precautionary fix surfaced during the static-pass investigation of Mike's playtest regressions (`context/scratch/regression-investigation-2026-04-24.md`); it MAY OR MAY NOT address the reported "weapon invisible in Campaign + CS" symptom. Bug class: `forgeTransitionToInactive` calls `forgeRestorePlayerMode` only when `forgeCurrentPlayer()` returns non-NULL (`src/game/forgemode.c:369-372`); `forgeRestorePlayerMode` itself further guards the body-restore on `p->prop && p->prop->chr` (`forgemode.c:179`). When the inactive transition fires while the player chr is mid-teardown (typical on stage->TITLE transition), one or both guards skip; `s_forge.fly.has_saved_movemode` and / or `has_saved_body` stay true with stale `saved_*` values. The dangling chr is gone so the stale data never lands on a live chr in practice, but a future code path that reads the saved fields without re-validating would observe stale state. Fix: unconditionally clear both flags at the top of `forgeTransitionToInactive` regardless of player / chr state. Inactive transition is now idempotent. Integer `saved_*` fields left at last-known values (every consumer guards on the flag; clearing them was unnecessary churn). Also audited the codebase for stale ACTION_COUNT references after the 62 -> 68 bump in `29d05d49` (Issue 8b sidebar): all consumers reference the symbol; only literal hits on `62` are unrelated (chr-anim table data, F5 keyboard scancode, comment labels, sha256 buffer index). No stale hard-coded ACTION_COUNT values found. | `src/game/forgemode.c::forgeTransitionToInactive` (defensive flag reset block before the existing state assignment) | **Verify the fix is harmless:** (1) Enter Grid, exit cleanly via menu -> the existing happy path still restores chr->bodynum and chr->headnum correctly; flags are false post-transition. (2) Enter Grid, force a stage -> TITLE transition while in FREEFLY -> log shows `GRID: -> INACTIVE (stage left gameplay)`; subsequent Grid entry does not skip the body-swap save (because flags are false now, not stuck true). (3) Defensive nature means the absence of "broken thing happened" is the success condition; nothing observable should change in normal flow. **Does NOT claim to fix the regression Mike reported** -- if the weapon-invisible symptom persists in playtest after this lands, the root cause is elsewhere. |

Cross-platform fallback (M-2.4) verified by code review -- the loader's manifest read does not depend on the OS shell. **What needs Mike's manual hand on a real install:** first-boot migration verification, Property Handler registration via `tools/pdmod_prophandler/install/register.ps1`, Explorer Properties dialog showing populated fields, in-game mod-manager hot-toggle smoke test, trust-gate verification with a synthetic `mods/shared/test_friend/` archive. Verification matrix file enumerates each step with PASS / READY status and code citations. **Original design notes preserved below for reference.** Mike's architectural extension to Q18: `.pdmod` becomes the **canonical mod format for the entire system**, not only for shared / transferred mods. Mod tools output `.pdmod` (not loose folder trees), the mods folder contains `.pdmod` files, the loader interprets them at runtime through a virtual file system without on-disk extraction. Sharing surfaces (chat, Public Mods Page) use the artifact already on disk -- no repackaging step. Folder-tidiness goal: post-migration `data/mods/installed/` contains one file per mod. Mike addendum (file-system metadata): a Windows Property Handler DLL ships with the installer to expose `mod.json` headline fields (creator / version / description / tags) at the OS shell layer; right-click Properties in Explorer and column views render them without opening the archive. Loader uses the same path for fast-scan startup (large speedup for users with many mods). Defensive zip-comment mirror lets non-Windows tools (7-Zip, Linux file managers) read the same headlines. Single source of truth: `mod.json` inside the archive; OS-shell metadata is a projection. Phasing: M-1 dual-support loader + Property Handler + benchmark; M-2 mod tools migrate (Theme tool first); M-3 one-shot auto-package of legacy folder mods on first run after upgrade; M-4 (optional, contingent on Mike) retire folder loader. Trust model continuity: the manual-install gate from B-237 / Q18 applies uniformly to locally-authored, chat-shared, and Public-Mods-Page-downloaded `.pdmod` files; only `mods/installed/` is loader-trusted; `mods/shared/` is read-only-for-browsing. Total estimate ~4-5 weeks of focused work. | Design: `context/designs/pdmod-unified-mod-format.md`. Companion: `context/designs/connectivity-and-modern-main-menu.md` Section 7 (format + sharing) plus the file-system-metadata cross-reference. | **B-237** | MED | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-04-24 (Issue 4b music speed-lerp).** Mike approved a music-sync upgrade so peers in the same MP match playing the same track stay in lock-step. Implementation: bump NET_PROTOCOL_VER 39 -> 40; SVC_MUSIC_ADVANCE wire format gains a u32 match_clock_offset_ms after the existing track_id string. Authoritative host (NETMODE_SERVER_AUDIO) re-broadcasts the same track every MUSIC_SYNC_REBROADCAST_MS (2000 ms) with the elapsed-since-track-start offset, plus the existing track-change broadcast on playlist advance. Clients route the packet to audioMusicSyncReceive (audio.c) which on a track-change starts the new track and hard-seeks to the host offset (late-joiners pick up mid-song), and on a same-track receive caches the (recv_wallclock, host_offset_at_recv) pair for the correction tick. audioMusicSyncCorrectionTick runs once per audio frame from the end of audioEndFrame: it computes drift = expected_host_offset_now - local_position_ms, then either holds rate at 1.0 (drift inside +-30 ms dead-band), lerps rate via clamp(1.0 + drift_ms*0.001, 0.97, 1.03) for moderate drift, or hard-seeks to the host offset when drift exceeds +-5000 ms. modmusic backend rewrote the integer-cursor mix loop to use a fractional cursor with linear interpolation between adjacent stereo frames so sub-sample rate changes work cleanly; rate is reset to 1.0 on every modMusicPlay / Stop. New API: modMusicSetRate / GetRate, modMusicGetPositionMs / SetPositionMs, modMusicGetDurationMs. Diagnostic: throttled "MUSIC.SYNC: drift=N ms rate=X.XX" log every 2 s when actively correcting, plus "MUSIC.SYNC: hard-seek..." when the band is exceeded. | `port/include/net/net.h` (NET_PROTOCOL_VER 40 + comment), `port/src/net/netmsg.c` (SVC_MUSIC_ADVANCE write/read + broadcast), `port/include/net/netmsg.h` (signature change), `port/src/audio.c` (host re-broadcast tick + audioMusicSyncReceive + audioMusicSyncCorrectionTick), `port/src/modmusic.c` (fractional-cursor mix loop + rate/position API), `port/include/modmusic.h` (new API), `port/include/audio.h` (sync entry points), `port/src/server_stubs.c` (no-op stubs for pd-server) | **Playtest:** (1) Two listen-host clients in the same MP match: start a track on the host, observe both hear the track; check logs for "MUSIC.SYNC: drift=... rate=..." lines stabilising near rate=1.0. (2) Start a fresh client mid-track: track starts at the host's current offset, not from the beginning; check log for "MUSIC.SYNC: track-change -> '...' offset=N ms". (3) Force drift on the client (e.g. simulate a hitch via Audio.QueueLimit, or temporarily rate-disable the host re-broadcast): drift should exceed 5000 ms and the client should hard-seek -- log "MUSIC.SYNC: hard-seek (drift=... exceeded)". (4) Pitch shift at +-3% rate: audible if listening for it but transient -- the lerp deadband of +-30 ms and gain K=0.001 means rates only push toward the boundaries during sustained drift. |
| **B-236** | HIGH | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-04-24 (AUDIT-24-H2 + H3 closure).** 4-24 super audit traced that `JBTN_BACK -> ACTION_FORGE_TOGGLE`, `JOFS_RTRIG -> ACTION_FORGE_ASCEND`, and `JOFS_LTRIG -> ACTION_FORGE_DESCEND` were dual-bound in `g_ImcGameplay` alongside SCORECARD / FIRE_PRIMARY / FIRE_SECONDARY. `fireVk` (port/src/actionmap.cpp:469) is single-winner-per-IMC: lowest trigger slot, then lowest action enum on tie. The Forge actions all had higher enum ids and lost every press — gamepad Back never toggled Forge mode, gamepad triggers never raised/lowered the freefly camera. Same root caused LSHIFT/LCTRL Forge BOOST/PRECISION to lose to SPRINT/CROUCH on keyboard too. Fix: split into two new IMCs. `g_ImcForgeSession` (priority 6, active for whole forge session) hosts `JBTN_BACK -> FORGE_TOGGLE`; the existing `g_ImcForge` (priority 7, FREEFLY only) gains the LT/RT triggers and LSHIFT/LCTRL modifier binds. Gameplay IMC retains keyboard F7/E/Q (each is the sole binding for its key, no collision). With this split: outside session = original gameplay behaviour; in NORMAL playtest = Back enters FREEFLY, every other gameplay action keeps working; in FREEFLY = forge IMC shadows triggers/modifiers/D-pad/LB/RB/X for camera + sidebar control. Sticks are unaffected (axis polling bypasses IMC priority entirely). | `port/src/actionmap.cpp` (new `g_ImcForgeSession` + `setupForgeSessionDefaults`; bindings moved out of `setupGameplayDefaults` into `setupForgeDefaults` / `setupForgeSessionDefaults`), `port/include/actionmap.h` (extern decl), `src/game/forgemode.c` (`imcActivate(&g_ImcForgeSession)` on session entry, `imcDeactivate` on inactive transition) | **Playtest:** (1) Outside any forge session: Back shows scorecard, RT fires primary, LT fires secondary, LSHIFT sprints, LCTRL crouches — unchanged. (2) Enter Grid; you start in FREEFLY: Back returns to NORMAL playtest. RT raises camera, LT lowers it. LSHIFT boosts speed, LCTRL slows. X toggles sidebar. LB/RB cycle tabs. Sticks fly normally. (3) In NORMAL playtest within a session: Back re-enters FREEFLY. RT/LT fire weapons (gameplay). LSHIFT/LCTRL = sprint/crouch. X = USE / interact. LB/RB = WEAPON_PREV/NEXT. (4) Pause menu in FREEFLY: pause IMC priority 11 wins; Back closes pause. |

S306 added the `pdguiConsumeTitleClose` direct channel for the X-button case; this fix adds the parallel `actionPressed(0, ACTION_CANCEL_USE)` channel for the B-button case. `actionPressed` is hardware-level and cannot be consumed by ImGui's nav. The `closeGracePending` (150ms B-131 grace) guard is kept for the `IsKeyPressed` keyboard-Escape path only (that one still needs protection against queued opening-press edges); the new `actionCancelEdge` and existing `titleClose` paths are gated only by `!IsWindowAppearing()`. | `port/fast3d/pdgui_menu_mainmenu.cpp::renderMainMenu` close block around line 4062 | **Playtest:** Main Menu open, controller B: closes on first press. Keyboard Escape: closes on first press (unchanged). Mouse X: closes on first click (unchanged). Rapid open-close-open-close cycle: no lost or double-fired close. Settings / Modding / Online Play sub-views: B returns to the top-level main menu on first press. |
| **B-229** | HIGH | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-04-23.** Mike: "I set the spawn weapon to remote mine, in the match start it says I have Falcon (Silenced), though it isn't equipped either." Root cause: the B-181 fallback block in `setup.c` ("too few map pickups -> force spawn-with-weapon") was unconditionally overriding `g_MatchConfig.spawn_weapon_id` with the first valid weapon in the configured set, even when the user had explicitly selected a specific spawn weapon. User picked `base:remotemine` (spawnWeaponNum=34); with Chicago's markers=0 the fallback fired and replaced it with MPWEAPON_FALCON2 (which in the user's catalog resolved to Falcon Silencer). Fix: gate the override on `spawnWeaponNum == 0xFF` (random) OR `== 0` (unset). Only fire the "fill from set" logic when the user has no explicit preference. When the user has explicitly picked a weapon, MPOPTION_SPAWNWITHWEAPON is still enabled (so pickup-less arenas still arm players) but the user's choice is preserved. Log the preservation decision with `SETUP: user-picked spawn weapon '%s' num=%d preserved`. Pairs with B-219 v3 setup.c preload so any chosen spawn weapon has its FP model loaded regardless of map markers. | `src/game/setup.c` B-181 fallback block (around line 2483) | **Playtest:** (1) Chicago CS, spawn weapon = remote mine, start match: player spawns with remote mine visible, equipped, fires correctly. Log shows `SETUP: user-picked spawn weapon 'base:remotemine' num=34 preserved`. (2) Chicago CS, spawn weapon = random: fallback fires, first valid set weapon used, log shows `SETUP: random spawn weapon resolved from set: id='base:...' num=N`. (3) Arena WITH markers (Temple, Skedar): spawn-with-weapon not forced, map pickups still place normally. |
| **B-228a** | MED | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-04-24 (Option E -- transport-prop visibility).** Mike approved Option E from the audit's correction. After the main props loop in `setupCreateProps` (src/game/setup.c) runs against the MP setup blob, an additional pass loads the SP setup blob via `assetLoadToNew(stage.setup_handle, ...)` for the SP-in-MP class stages (CITRAINING, CHICAGO, VILLA, INFILTRATION, G5BUILDING, PELAGIC). The pass walks the SP props, accepts ONLY `OBJTYPE_LIFT` and `OBJTYPE_ESCASTEP`, and runs the same creation logic the main MP switch uses (lift accel/maxspeed unpack, modeldef preload, geocount, setupCreateObject, liftUpdateTiles, auto-register against `g_Lifts[]` via the lift's pad's liftnum; escastep matrix setup). `g_StageSetup.props` is temporarily repointed at the SP blob during this pass so `setupGetCmdByIndex` resolves SP-internal door cross-references correctly, then restored. The SP blob lives in `MEMPOOL_STAGE` and is reclaimed at stage unload, keeping `lift->doors[i]` pointers (which reference SP-blob `doorobj` structs) valid for the stage lifetime. We do NOT load the SP doors as live props -- the lift moves and players ride; door coupling reads coords from the doorobj struct fields (sufficient for tile extents) without animated open/close. Diagnostic: `SETUP.LIFT: SP-in-MP stagenum=0x%02x lifts=N escasteps=M`. No protocol change, no `pd-server` impact (server_stubs.c covers the load path; setup.c is client-only). | `src/game/setup.c::setupCreateProps` (new SP-overlay block after the main props loop, around line 2503+) | **Playtest matrix:** Per the readiness audit (`context/audits/sp-stage-mp-readiness-2026-04-24.md`), CI Training is the canonical target -- previously had no working lifts in MP. After Option E lands, host CI as a CS arena and confirm the lifts at the firing range / training rooms move when stepped on; log shows `SETUP.LIFT: SP-in-MP stagenum=0x59 lifts=N escasteps=0`. Repeat for Chicago / G5Building (which also have SP lifts on stair-traversal). Airbase is the fattest target (5 lifts + 16 doors + 40 escasteps) but lives behind a different stagenum class -- queued for a future audit if needed. Verify no regression on stages NOT in the SP-in-MP class (Combat Sim arenas like Skedar / Felicity / Temple should behave identically -- no SP overlay because their stagenum doesn't match). |

| **B-228-historic** | MED | **REOPENED 2026-04-24 (P8 diagnosis correction).** P5 (commit `216fd27f`) shipped a `mptransport_diffflag` relax that turns off the `OBJFLAG2_EXCLUDE_2P/3P/4P` + difficulty filter for `OBJTYPE_LIFT` / `OBJTYPE_ESCASTEP` on SP-in-MP class stages. P8 static audit (`context/audits/sp-stage-mp-readiness-2026-04-24.md`) counted `lift(` / `escastep(` macros in every SP and MP setup C file and found the MP setups (`mp_setupdish.c`, `mp_setuppete.c`, `mp_setupeld.c`, `mp_setuplue.c`, `mp_setupdepo.c`, `mp_setupdam.c`) are EMPTY of those macros. SP setups contain the lifts (`setupdish.c` = 2 lifts + 8 doors, `setuplue.c` = 4 lifts + 8 doors, `setupcave.c` = 5 lifts + 16 doors + 40 escasteps, etc.). P5 is therefore a NOOP on the current codebase -- the filter rejects nothing because nothing is loaded to filter. P5 is still structurally correct as insurance (a future mod adding a lift to an MP setup with exclude bits would benefit) and the mptransport_diffflag path is kept. | Real fix sketch (Option E): in `setupLoadStage`, after loading the MP setup blob, for SP-in-MP class stages additionally load the SP setup via `assetLoadToNew(stage.setup_handle, ...)`, iterate its props, and run ONLY `OBJTYPE_LIFT` + `OBJTYPE_ESCASTEP` cases through the existing switch. Pad references inside lifts are stage-bound (same `padsfileid` for SP and MP), so they resolve cleanly. Costs: one extra setup blob in memory for those stages; no wire protocol change; no `pd-server` impact (server_stubs.c covers the load path). | **Playtest:** see the SP-setup transport-prop inventory in the readiness matrix for which stages the Option E fix would repopulate (Airbase is the fattest target at 5+16+40; Chicago / Villa / G5Building / Pelagic / CrashSite have zero SP lifts so Option E is a noop there too -- Chicago's fire-escape stairs are bg_pete tiles, not objects). |

| **B-218** | MED | **FIXED v2 PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-04-23.** 4-21 fix (`ORCH_RELAX_ITERS` 10->20, `min_sep` floor 35->50, post-relax duplicate detect + `botSpawn` live-spawn fallback on XZ proximity) addressed the symptom but not the root cause. Playtest 2026-04-23: bots still all spawned at one point on the initial match frame, respawn fine. Root cause traced: `mpOrchestrateMatchStartSpawns` runs from `lv.c` between the `playerReset` loop and the `playerSpawn` loop (so player match-start positions apply before `playerSpawn`). At that point bots are allocated as chrs but the orchestrator participant iteration in `mpspawn_orchestrate.c:408-420` sees empty `g_MpBotChrPtrs[i]->prop` and skips every bot. Orchestrator places ONLY players, sets `g_MpOrchestrateInitialSpawnDone = true`, and leaves every `g_MpOrchestrateBotPoolIdx[]` entry at -1. When `botSpawnAll` fires later (via AI-script `aiMpInitSimulants` opcode 0x0185) bots have valid props, but the orchestrator branch in `botSpawn` (~line 338) fails the `>= 0` check and falls through to `scenarioChooseSpawnLocation` at line 393. Called in rapid succession for 32 bots in the same tick, that function repeatedly returns the same first-valid pad -> pile-up. Respawns use the tiered pool (`spawnPoolSelectTiered`) and are unaffected. v2 fix: top of `botSpawnAll` now detects the "no bot has an orchestrated pool idx" state and re-runs `mpOrchestrateReset` + `mpOrchestrateMatchStartSpawns` so bots get their Hungarian-assigned slots now that props exist. Player positions remain stable because the orchestrator is deterministic on same seed + roster + stage. | `src/game/bot.c::botSpawnAll` (re-run guard at top), secondary: `src/game/mplayer/mpspawn_orchestrate.c` (reset preserves player pool placement by design) | Chicago / high-bot matches: log now shows `SPAWN.ORCH: botSpawnAll re-running orchestrator (bots missed initial pass, g_BotCount=N)` followed by the Hungarian placement log. Initial match frame: bots dispersed across the pool. `SPAWN.DIAG: post-botSpawnAll` variance WARNING no longer fires. Regression check: respawn after death continues to use tiered pool and remains dispersed. |

| **B-199** | CRITICAL | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-04-19 (happy-blackburn-8eb2ff)**. `0xc0000005 ACCESS_VIOLATION` crashing the game 80 ms after loading Investigation (stagenum `0x33`, bgfile 31 `bg_ear.seg`). addr2line stack: `func0f00505c dlights.c:1836 → func0f004c6c dlights.c:1787 → lightsReset dlights.c:561 → lvReset lv.c:615 → mainLoop pdmain.c:594`. Crash at `var8006142c[portalnum2][portalnum]` with out-of-range `portalnum2` read from a garbage `g_RoomPortals[]` entry. Root cause: the B-185 safety in `bgLoadFile` (fbc276ce) used a single `memset(memaddr, 0, len)` on any `len > romsize - offset` condition — which fires for every call whose `len` is rounded up to 16-byte alignment even when the final 1–15 bytes are just off-file padding. Investigation's `bg_ear.seg` section 3 is 1254 bytes at offset 195850 but the aligned read length is 1264 (`((section3compsize-1)|0xf)+1`); file ends at 197104, so `offset+len` overshoots by exactly 10 bytes. The aggressive `memset` destroyed all 1254 valid bytes of the compressed portal blob, `bgInflate` then produced zeroes, and `g_Rooms[]` / `g_RoomPortals[]` came back with invalid portal indices. Triggered specifically on Investigation because `bg_ear.seg` section 3 is not 16-byte-padded in the ROM; other stages happened to be aligned. Pre-existing latent since B-185 (01:35, 2026-04-19); surfaced during Wave 1 Super Audit playtest when user loaded Investigation. Fix: `bgLoadFile` split into three paths — (1) `offset >= romsize` → zero all (offset past EOF, no valid data); (2) `len > romsize - offset` → partial read — `memcpy(memaddr, src+offset, romsize-offset)` then zero only the padding bytes; only log an ERROR when the missing bytes exceed 16 (alignment gap is silent, real corruption still logs); (3) else → full memcpy. Preserves B-185's deterministic-zero intent for true failure modes while letting aligned reads succeed with their real data. Build clean 2/2 (PerfectDark + PerfectDarkServer). | `src/game/bg.c` | Load Combat Sim → **Investigation** (stagenum 0x33). Stage should load without crashing in `func0f00505c`. No `BG.LOAD: partial over-read` ERROR should appear for Investigation's section 3 load (the 10-byte gap is alignment padding ≤ 16, below the warn threshold). Regression check: load Complex, Skedar Ruins, Villa, Temple, Sewers, Crashsite — every stage should still load correctly (aligned sections follow the full-`memcpy` path). If any stage logs `BG.LOAD: partial over-read ... copied=N` with a gap > 16 bytes, that's real data corruption in the seg file and needs separate triage. |
| **B-198** | HIGH | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-04-19 (infallible-goldberg-71b379 — S385)**. Combat Simulator End Game confirm + end-of-match input bug, two related issues in one entry. (1) CS pause → "End Game" row: controller activated the row and opened the DANGER confirm popup, but controller nav couldn't reach the red "End Match" / Confirm button — focus didn't latch onto Cancel on the popup's first frame (BeginPopupModal + `OpenPopup` same-frame race with ImGui's popup NavInit), and D-pad Right moved nothing. (2) CS end-of-match screen: after a match ended naturally (timer/score limit), the endscreen rendered but all input was dead — Escape/Enter/A/B did nothing, player couldn't return to the CI lobby. Root cause for (2) was a race between `menuPushRootDialog`'s `menupoolReleaseAll()` (which schedules deferred pops of any owned_ctx) and `renderMpEndscreen`'s fresh-entry gate: on the renderer's first frame `inputCtxIsActive(&g_CtxImGuiMenu)` still returned true (deferred pop not yet fired) so the push was skipped; end-of-frame the pop fired; next frame `freshEntry` was false so no re-push — `g_ImcMenu` never activated, so `ACTION_USE`/`ACTION_CANCEL_USE` couldn't produce ImGui Enter/Escape events. Compounding: `g_MpEndscreenIndGameOverMenuDialog` / `TeamGameOverMenuDialog` / `ChallengeCompletedMenuDialog` were unregistered in the menu pool (only Cheated + Failed were), so their push went through the unregistered fallback with no pool ctx management. Fix: (1) register all five MP endscreen dialogs + `g_MpEndGameMenuDialog` (as WARNING_MODAL) in `port/src/menupool.c`. (2) `renderMpEndscreen` now pushes `g_CtxImGuiMenu` unconditionally whenever it isn't active, not just on fresh entry — force-close sites also clear the dialog from the legacy menu stack, so there's no resurrect-loop risk. (3) `renderMpEndGameDialog` gains an `s_EndGameOpenFrame` counter: `SetKeyboardFocusHere(0)` before Cancel for the first 5 frames after `OpenPopup` forces focus onto Cancel regardless of popup NavInit timing, and button clicks + keyboard shortcuts are gated on a 3-frame debounce so the Enter/A press that opened the popup can't bleed through. All popup-close paths clear the frame counter for clean re-entry. Build clean 775/775. `PerfectDark.exe` 53,363,195 / `PerfectDarkServer.exe` 23,141,856. | `port/src/menupool.c`, `port/fast3d/pdgui_menu_endscreen.cpp`, `port/fast3d/pdgui_menu_warning.cpp` | (1) CS pause → "End Game" with controller: popup appears centered over scrim, focus ring on Cancel. D-pad Right moves focus to "End Match". A confirms. B/Esc cancels. First 3 frames reject clicks + shortcuts (bleed-through debounce). Keyboard: arrows/Enter/Esc/Space all work. (2) Play CS match to natural end (timer/score): endscreen appears with rankings. Enter → Play Again. Esc → Main Menu. Controller A/B mirror those via `pdguiDriveImGuiNav`. (3) Challenge mode regression: Completed/Failed/Cheated endscreens all still work (same renderer now covers all five variants through pool registration). (4) No new WARNINGs in pd-client.log related to `MENUPOOL: unregistered dialogdef` for endscreen / end game dialogs. |
| **B-197** | CRITICAL | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-04-19 (brave-bouman-13bd68)**. `ACCESS_VIOLATION` at `PC=0x7ff7bc5cef4a (+0x21ef4a)` crashing the game during CI (stage `0x26`) free-roam, ~1.5s after the user closed the main menu and started walking. Tester's log at `[00:22.99]`: `FATAL: ACCESS_VIOLATION PC=0x7ff7bc5cef4a (+0x21ef4a) CODE=0xc0000005 MODULE=0x7ff7bc3b0000`. Last breadcrumb `CHR.TICK slot=0 chrnum=0 action=1 race=0 model=…` at `22.992183s` followed by crash. addr2line resolves the stack to `gfx_sp_matrix port/fast3d/gfx_pc.cpp:1093 → gfx_run_dl gfx_pc.cpp:2489 (G_MTX case) → gfx_run gfx_pc.cpp:2900 → videoSubmitCommands video.c:133 → schedSubmitTask pdsched.c:241 → rdpCreateTask rdp.c:99 → mainTick pdmain.c:780`. Root cause: `gfx_sp_matrix` at line 1093 does `int32_t int_part = addr[i*2+j/2];` where `addr` is `seg_addr(cmd->words.w1)` decoded from a `G_MTX` display-list command in a BG room's GDL. When a room's display list was partially clamped by the B-195 bounds fix (`port/src/preprocess/gbi.c::gbiConvertGdl` + `convertRoomGfxData` — added this week), or when the N64 ROM data references a matrix via a cross-room offset that the PC port's per-room inflated buffer can't satisfy, the decoded pointer can land on NULL-adjacent memory. First `addr[...]` deref is an AV. Same pointer class as B-195 (Complex bg room preprocess overflow) — B-195 guarded the *preprocess* (allocation / DL conversion) side; B-197 guards the *render* (consumer) side. Unrelated to B-196 (imgui focus leak, also in the same session): log confirms B-196 fix worked — `MENUPOOL: released main_menu gen=1` + `INPUTCTX: imgui_menu on_pop` cascade fired cleanly at `[00:21.43]`, then `[00:22.57] DIAG axis: move=0.000,-1.000 back=1` shows WASD responsive 1s later, and the crash occurred 400ms after that while the player was walking backward. Fix: at the top of `gfx_sp_matrix` (`port/fast3d/gfx_pc.cpp`), early-return with an identity matrix when `(uintptr_t)addr < 0x10000` — any legitimate Win64 user pointer is ≥ 64 KB; anything below is guaranteed garbage. Logs `WARNING: B-197: gfx_sp_matrix addr=%p too low — skipping (corrupt BG display list? params=0x%02x, warn N/5)` capped at 5 warnings per session. Downstream matrix stack stays consistent (identity push is a no-op visually), game stays alive. Complements (doesn't replace) the B-195 preprocess bounds clamps — together they cover the full pipeline from inflated ROM data → GDL conversion → render. | `port/fast3d/gfx_pc.cpp` (guarded top of `gfx_sp_matrix`) | Play Combat Sim → Carrington Institute (stage 0x26) → close Agent Select / Main Menu → walk around with WASD for 30+ seconds. No AV at PC+0x21ef4a; game stays alive even if a corrupt G_MTX is encountered. Log may show one or more `WARNING: B-197: gfx_sp_matrix addr=…` lines (capped at 5) identifying corrupt-matrix events — affected rooms may render with one draw call using identity transform (minor visual glitch) but the scene continues. Regression-check: Complex, Skedar Ruins, Temple — no render-path crashes on any stage load. |
| **B-196** | HIGH | **FIXED — PLAYTEST CONFIRMED 2026-04-19 (brave-bouman-13bd68)**. Post-fix tester log `Build/pd-client.log` (03:56) confirms fix works: `[00:21.42] MENU_IMGUI: main menu CLOSE via ESC/B` → `[00:21.43] MENUPOOL: released main_menu gen=1 ctx=imgui_menu` → `[00:21.43] INPUTCTX: imgui_menu on_pop -- g_ImcMenu deactivated` cascade. At `[00:22.57] DIAG poll: topCtx='gameplay' menuActive=0 numActiveIMCs=1` + `DIAG axis: move=0.000,-1.000 back=1` — **WASD registered as backward movement**, previously dead. No `B-195: ImGui WantCaptureKeyboard=1` WARNING in the subsequent 1.5s of free-roam (game crashed 400ms later due to the unrelated B-197 gfx_sp_matrix bug, not anything input-related). (User originally filed conceptually as a follow-on to B-194; renumbered B-196 here because dev's B-195 was already allocated to the Complex-stage crash fix from `frosty-banach-0e3fc1`.) Input stuck: after closing a menu cold-boot (Agent Select → Main Menu → close with Esc), WASD / Space no longer move the player in CI free-roam. Only the pause/menu-toggle key (Escape / Start) still functions, and pressing it visibly "doubles up" the menu (opens and closes strangely). Distinct from B-194 (Agent Select ctx leak) which showed zero `MENUPOOL:` lines and a stuck `imgui_menu` ctx — B-194 is confirmed fixed in this session (clean `MENUPOOL: acquired/released` + `INPUTCTX: imgui_menu on_pop` cascade at `[00:14.63]`-`[00:14.64]` of tester log `Build/pd-client.log`). B-196 only manifests *after* the ctx pops cleanly. Tester's 03:32 log shows a clean close cascade at `[00:14.63]`-`[00:14.64]` with `INPUTCTX: imgui_menu on_pop` firing and gameplay IMC becoming top. In the ensuing 8 seconds of free-roam (until the user re-pressed pause at `[00:22.48] MENU: ACTION_PAUSE detected ... lvframenum=945`) `BMOVE: allowc1x=1 ... AXIS_MOVE=0.000,0.000` stays all-zero despite the input gate being open — movement keys never reached the action map. Root cause: `pdguiProcessEvent` in `port/fast3d/pdgui_backend.cpp:849-859` routes keyboard events through the game's actionmap **only if `!ImGui::GetIO().WantCaptureKeyboard`** (Escape and Enter are whitelisted through regardless). ImGui's `WantCaptureKeyboard` is derived from `NavWindow` / `NavId` / `ActiveId`, and these references can survive a menu's final `Begin`/`End` teardown — the window is gone but ImGui still remembers the last-focused widget as the nav anchor. With `WantCaptureKeyboard` stuck at 1 while the input-ctx stack is back at `gameplay`, WASD / Space get silently swallowed by the gate, and the next Esc press drives ImGui's nav action (causing the "menu doubling up" appearance because ImGui-side focus bounces through the phantom nav while the game's ACTION_PAUSE also fires). Fix — two parts: (1) new `pdguiClearImGuiFocusAndNav()` helper in `port/fast3d/pdgui_backend.cpp` calls `ImGui::FocusWindow(nullptr)` + `ImGui::ClearActiveID()`; (2) `imguiMenuOnPop` in `port/src/inputctx.c` invokes the helper right before the existing `imcDeactivate` + log line. Fires on the very last imgui menu close (when the shared `imgui_menu` input context pops back to depth 0), which is exactly when ImGui should release its keyboard claim. Belt-and-suspenders leak-class catcher: `pdguiProcessEvent` emits a rate-limited `WARNING: B-195: ImGui WantCaptureKeyboard=1 while top ctx is gameplay — swallowing sym=0x...` if the gate ever swallows a key while no menu is active, so any future regression of this leak class surfaces in the log instead of silently breaking WASD again (WARNING text retains the B-195 tag from the original filing — harmless, just a bug-id mismatch in the log string). | `port/fast3d/pdgui_backend.cpp`, `port/include/pdgui.h`, `port/src/inputctx.c` | Cold boot → CI free-roam appears (B-193 repro or clean) → open Agent Select → sign in or back out → open Main Menu → close with Esc → immediately press W/A/S/D → **player moves** (previously dead). Space jumps. Esc reopens the menu without the "doubling" flicker. Log should show `INPUTCTX: imgui_menu on_pop -- g_ImcMenu deactivated` on close; NO `WARNING: B-195: ImGui WantCaptureKeyboard=1 while top ctx is gameplay` in the subsequent free-roam. Repeat for 5 open/close cycles. For textbox flows (Interface theme search, Modding Hub filename entry): WASD STILL doesn't move the player while the textbox is focused — that's correct behaviour (gate still fires because imgui legitimately wants the keyboard); Esc closes the textbox, then WASD works again. |
| **B-195** | HIGH | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-04-19 (frosty-banach-0e3fc1)**. Hard crash (`sysFatalError`) loading **Complex** (stagenum `0x1f`, `bg_ref.seg` / `bg_ref_tilesZ`) in Combat Sim solo (1 player, 0 bots). Crash log `Build/pdclient-crash-complex-apr19.log` tail: stage setup reports success (`LOAD: bgReset done` + `LOAD: bgBuildTables done` at `[00:24.92]`, `SETUP: setupLoadStage complete`, `LV.DIAG: first-render stage=0x1f roomcount=45 player_room=7 camera_room=7 pos=(-3817,159,349) frame=0`) — then on the first tick's room GFX load: `ERROR: FATAL: overflow when trying to preprocess a bg room, size 1152 newsize 15528` from `port/src/preprocess/filebg.c:472` (`preprocessBgRoom`'s `sysFatalError` on `newSize > size`). Build version `dev f32d1e51`. Root cause: Complex room 7's inflated gfx data was 576 bytes (printed `size 1152` is the internal post-doubled scratch size) and the PC-port 32→64-bit preprocessing produced 15528 bytes of output — a ~27× expansion that far exceeds the 2× scratch multiplier hardcoded in `preprocessBgRoom`. The legitimate per-room 64-bit pointer expansion is ~2×, so the runaway output was reads past the end of the per-room inflated buffer: on N64 the whole BG segment was a single contiguous blob so `roomblock.ptr_gdl` / `ptr_vertices` / `ptr_colours` could reference offsets anywhere in the segment; in the PC port each room is inflated into its own independent buffer by `bgInflate`, so any offset pointing past this room's data walks into adjacent garbage. `gbiConvertGdl` (port/src/preprocess/gbi.c) read until it found a byte matching `G_ENDDL` (0xB8) and wrote 2× (8 → 16 bytes/cmd) of host output for every read — ~485 bogus commands = 7764 src bytes read, 15528 dst bytes written — blowing the scratch and the caller's allocation. Three-layer fix: (1) `gbiConvertGdl` now takes an explicit `src_size` and synthesizes an ENDDL when a read would cross that boundary (logged `WARNING: gbiConvertGdl: source overrun at srcpos=… src_size=…`). (2) `convertRoomGfxData` clamps `endpos` / `vtx_end` / `col_end` to the actual inflated `src_size` and skips any `ptr_gdl` whose offset is outside the buffer (logged `WARNING: convertRoomGfxData: gdl[i] src_offset=… outside src_size=… — skipping`). (3) `preprocessBgRoom` bumps the scratch dst allocation from 2× → 8× with a 4 KB floor; `sysFatalError` replaced with `LOG_ERROR` + `return 0` so a malformed room degrades rather than killing the process. `src/game/bg.c`'s caller allocation bumped from 2× → 8× with a 16 KB floor so the `memcpy(data, dst, newSize)` back into the allocation cannot overflow. Caller guard added: `if (inflatedlen == 0)` skips room load cleanly. Callers `filemodel.c:660` updated to pass `src_file_len`. `port/src/preprocess/gbi.c` now includes `system.h` for `sysLogPrintf`. | `port/include/preprocess/gbi.h`, `port/src/preprocess/gbi.c`, `port/src/preprocess/filebg.c`, `port/src/preprocess/filemodel.c`, `src/game/bg.c` | Boot build, play Combat Sim → Complex (solo, 1 player, 0 bots). Stage must load and render all 45 rooms without the `FATAL: overflow when trying to preprocess a bg room` crash. Log may include `WARNING: convertRoomGfxData: gdl[i] src_offset=… outside src_size=…` or `WARNING: gbiConvertGdl: source overrun at srcpos=… src_size=…` for the specific room(s) whose ROM data referenced cross-room GDL offsets — those rooms may render with minor visual glitches (missing a GDL) but the game stays alive. Repeat on other stages (Temple, Ravine, G5 Building, Skedar Ruins) to regression-check stage loading. |
| **B-194** | HIGH | **FIXED — PLAYTEST CONFIRMED 2026-04-19 (brave-bouman-13bd68)**. Post-fix tester log `Build/pd-client.log` (03:07) shows the clean acquire/release pair: `[00:15.75] MENUPOOL: acquired agent_select gen=1 def=… ctx=none(shared)` → `[00:15.77] INPUTCTX: pushed 'imgui_menu' (depth now 2)` + `MENUPOOL: attached ctx to active agent_select gen=1 ctx=imgui_menu`; then on close `[00:18.70] MENUPOOL: released agent_select gen=1 ctx=imgui_menu` → `[00:18.72] INPUTCTX: imgui_menu on_pop -- g_ImcMenu deactivated`. Input returns to gameplay cleanly on back-out. (User originally filed as B-192; renumbered B-194 here to avoid collision with dev's B-191/B-193.) Input stuck after backing out of cold-boot Agent Select / main menu at CI. Repro: boot game → title intro skips to CI (stage 0x26) → press Start/Esc → Agent Select appears → back out without signing in → player can neither move nor reopen any menu until the process is killed. Tester log `Build/pdclient-apr19-0236.log`: `INPUTCTX: pushed 'imgui_menu' (depth now 2)` at `[00:16.32]` followed by `PREFS.AGENT: prefs reset to defaults (Agent Select open)` at `[00:16.33]`, but **zero `MENUPOOL: acquired …` or `MENUPOOL: released …` lines appear for the entire 7-second menu session**, and no matching `INPUTCTX: imgui_menu on_pop` before `shutdown` at `[00:23.60]`. Meanwhile `BMOVE: allowc1x=1 allowc1y=1 allowc1buttons=1` starts firing at `[00:19.40]` (game thinks input is allowed), yet `DIAG poll: topCtx='imgui_menu' menuActive=1` stays active until shutdown — so bmove-level gating says "allowed" but the input-context stack still routes all input to the menu IMC at priority 10 instead of gameplay. Root cause: `g_FilemgrFileSelectMenuDialog` (the non-4MB Agent Select dialogdef in `src/game/filemgr.c:3428`, the only one actually pushed on modern PC builds — `menuPushRootDialog(&g_FilemgrFileSelectMenuDialog, MENUROOT_FILEMGR)` at `filemgr.c:2818` in `filemgrConsiderPushingFileSelectDialog`) was **never registered with the menu pool**. `port/src/menupool.c:500` only registered the `4Mb` twin `g_FilemgrFileSelect4MbMenuDialog` (plus `g_PakChoosePakMenuDialog` and `g_ChangeAgentMenuDialog`). Effect: when `renderAgentSelect`'s `IsWindowAppearing` calls `menupoolAcquireDialog(&g_FilemgrFileSelectMenuDialog, &g_CtxImGuiMenu)`, `lookupType()` returns `MENU_TYPE_NONE`, taking the unregistered-fallback branch at menupool.c:275-283 which silently `inputCtxPush(ctx)` (no `MENUPOOL: acquired` log — matches the tester's zero-line fingerprint) and returns 1. On close (`menuPopDialog → menuCloseDialog → menupoolReleaseDialog`), the mirror branch at menupool.c:288-294 is a no-op for `MENU_TYPE_NONE`, so the pushed `g_CtxImGuiMenu` is orphaned. The menu IMC at priority 10 then permanently eats every ACTION_PAUSE / ACTION_MOVE / ACTION_USE keypress. Fix: **register `g_FilemgrFileSelectMenuDialog` as `MENU_TYPE_AGENT_SELECT`** in `port/src/menupool.c::menupoolInit`, matching the `4Mb` twin. The S304/S300 cascade `menuPopDialog → menuCloseDialog → menupoolReleaseDialog → inputCtxPopDeferred` then fires cleanly on every close path (Escape at agentselect.cpp:406, select-agent at :361/:368, X/delete, etc.) since the slot now has a registered type + owned ctx. Also hardened the unregistered-fallback leak class: `menupoolAcquireDialog` now emits `WARNING: MENUPOOL: unregistered dialogdef <ptr> pushed ctx <name> without pool dedup — add to menupoolInit to avoid ctx leak` and records the def pointer + ctx so the mirror `menupoolReleaseDialog` unregistered branch can `inputCtxPopDeferred` it. Any future unregistered dialog that pushes g_CtxImGuiMenu surfaces as a WARNING instead of silently stranding input. Build clean. | `port/src/menupool.c` (register FileSelect + unregistered-leak warning / deferred-pop) | Cold boot into CI → press Esc/Start → Agent Select appears → press Esc without signing in → CI regains input; Esc reopens Agent Select cleanly; WASD moves player. Log should now show `MENUPOOL: acquired agent_select gen=1 def=… ctx=imgui_menu(owned)` on open and `MENUPOOL: released agent_select gen=1 ctx=imgui_menu` followed by `INPUTCTX: imgui_menu on_pop -- g_ImcMenu deactivated` on close. | **B-193** | HIGH | **ROOT CAUSE IDENTIFIED + FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-04-19 (elated-hugle-7ec221 — S384)**. Commit `fe107e3e` (2026-04-17, M4 optimisation) collapsed `ALIGN16(val)` to `(val)` on the grounds that `mempAlloc` already returns aligned memory. That rationale held for SIZE-only uses but not for POINTER uses — `bg.c:1516` / `bg.c:1934` do `header = (u8 *)ALIGN16((uintptr_t)headerbuffer)` where `headerbuffer` is a `u8[0x50]` stack buffer that's meant to be advanced to a 16-aligned start before `preprocessBgSection{1,2,3}Header` parses structured fields out of it. With the no-op macro the parse runs from whatever offset the stack gave — on 64-bit the stack is usually 16-aligned so it MAY work (explaining the intermittent reproduction and the "not reproducing after a clean rebuild" brave-bouman observation on 5 cold boots — rebuilds shift stack frame layouts), but nothing guarantees it, and any function above in the call tree with misaligned locals shifts `headerbuffer` off. Identical unpatched pointer use at `bg.c:1537` (`scratch` inflate-buffer pointer) corrupts the primary-data buffer whenever the shift crosses a 16-byte lane. **Fix**: restore `ALIGN16(val)` to `((((val) + 0xf) | 0xf) ^ 0xf)` in `src/include/constants.h:79` so every pointer-alignment site gets a real round-up. 0–15 bytes of padding per allocation is trivial overhead on modern hardware. Prior status: intermittent, low-probability repro under incremental builds; brave-bouman-13bd68 Phase 5 diag (cam_pos/tickmode on frame 0+30) in `port/src/pdmain.c` retained for future regression-detection; frame-30 3-room CI output is the opening-cutscene TICKMODE_CUTSCENE which is correct-by-design (my earlier "3 rooms = broken" diagnosis was a false positive). Build clean 777/777. `PerfectDark.exe` 53,232,611 / `PerfectDarkServer.exe` 23,154,674. Renumbered from B-191 to B-193 to avoid collision with dev's Updater fix. | `src/include/constants.h` | Playtest: cold-boot CI 5× in a row — scene should render on every cold boot (no sky-only / invisible-geometry runs). LV.DIAG lines still emit at frame 0 and frame 30 so any future shortfall shows the exact state. |

Build gate: `spawnPoolBuildGlobal` runs only when `g_Vars.mplayerisrunning || g_NetMode != NETMODE_NONE` (playerreset.c:476). Most likely root causes — (1) Car Park's MP setup file has ≤1 INTROCMD_SPAWN pad AND a sparse waypoint graph, so the g_SpawnPoints[] fallback at playerreset.c:319 finds few valid entries; the pool then either doesn't build enough points or repeatedly picks the same entry as "farthest"; (2) all bots landing on the same pad then sharing `myaction = MA_AIBOTMAINLOOP` pathfind to the same scenario objective (king hill, ctc home pad, etc.) or default roam waypoint; (3) cascade from B-167 (non-skeletal props lacking bbox) leaving room/portal geometry unregistered so `bgFindRoomsByPos` fails for all spawn positions and bots fall back to the same void-recovery pad. Stuck-detection relocation at bot.c:1240 could also converge all bots on the SAME random-but-deterministic "max distance" waypoint (same rng seed → same target) — consistent with the "run to a specific spot" observation. **Partial mitigation + instrumentation shipped this session**: `botSpawnAll` now emits `SPAWN.DIAG:` dumps after the wave (per-bot pos/room) and a WARNING `SPAWN.DIAG: bot collision — bot[i] and bot[j] spawned %.0fu apart … spawn pipeline is collapsing to a single pad` when any pair is closer than 150u. (b) the new `SPAWN.DIAG: post-botSpawnAll stage=0x3d bots=N numpads=M` + per-bot pos lines, (c) the WARNING if the collision-variance check fires, (d) `SPAWN.TIER:` lines from the pool selector. Share log. |

| **B-172** | HIGH | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-04-18 (fervent-hertz-b709be)**. Server's `streamComponentToClient` resolves each via `assetCatalogResolve(mod_id)` -- mod IDs are not asset catalog IDs -- and logs `WARNING: DISTRIB: unknown catalog_id '<id>' requested`. Client marks each MISSING; ready gate never goes READY; user ESCs during the hang. ESC in Room falls through `mainEndStage` branch logic (no coop, no anti, `normmplayerisrunning=false` because the match never actually launched) and calls `endscreenPrepare()`, producing the `GAMELOOP.CAMPAIGN: endscreenPrepare stage=0x26` log line and stranding the user in a campaign-ended main menu with locked input. Fix (three layers): (1) `manifestBuild` and `manifestBuildForHost` (`port/src/net/netmanifest.c`) now skip mods where `!mod->valid \|\| !mod->has_modjson` -- audio-only mods are already represented via `MANIFEST_TYPE_AUDIO` playlist entries, so adding them as COMPONENT is redundant and stalls the gate; invalid mods can't be streamed. (2) `modmgrParseBotNames` (`port/src/modmgr.c`) early-returns when `!mod->has_modjson` to stop the boot-time `fsFileLoad ERROR` spam on every audio-only mod. (3) `mainEndStage` (`src/lib/main.c`) adds a new branch: if no match type is active (coop<0, anti<0, normmp=false) but `g_NetMode != 0`, log `GAMELOOP.CAMPAIGN: mainEndStage in networked lobby -- skipping endscreen, routing back to lobby` and skip `endscreenPrepare`. The room/lobby UI (or SVC_MATCH_CANCELLED → CLSTATE_LOBBY) handles the recovery state.
