| **B-165** | HIGH | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-04-18 (eager-benz — file.c `fileLoadRomToNew` stale `info->loadedsize` cache strips EXTRAMEM scratch on re-entry)**. Cache bug fix stands — still a real issue for repeated EXTRAMEM loads. But was NOT sufficient to fix the stage 0x26 torn-models symptom; see B-166 for the over-aggressive guard that was the actual cause of the user-visible symptom. This is the true root cause of B-161's "WHY models load torn in stage 0x26" open question. Repro: dev build `34636b53`, 64 MB memp, title loads OK, enter CI Training (0x26) from main menu — every body/head/prop in `MANIFEST-SP: applied diff — load=56 unload=0 keep=3` comes back torn (`MODELDEF: file N loaded torn — parts=0`). S323's `modeldefLoad` reject + S317/S327/S358 call-site guards correctly converted the AV into WARNING log lines, but the underlying load was still corrupted. Root cause: `src/game/file.c::fileLoadRomToNew` gated `info->loadedsize` recomputation on `loadedsize == 0`, but `fileLoad` overwrites `info->loadedsize` with the post-preprocess size on exit — stripping the `+0x8000` rzipInflate scratch buffer. On re-entry, `mempAlloc(info->loadedsize, MEMPOOL_STAGE)` allocates only the post-preprocess size; inside `fileLoad`, `scratch = dst + allocationlen - aligned(romsize)` falls inside the dst buffer. In-place inflate corrupts the deflate stream as the writer catches the reader → `rzipInflate` returns 0 → `romdataFilePreprocess` skips on `size==0` → dst stays zeroed → `modeldef->numparts=0`, `rootnode=NULL`, "loaded torn". Trigger chain: `catalogValidateAll()` at startup pre-loads every body/head through `safeModeldefLoad → modeldefLoadToNew → fileLoadRomToNew` (populates `loadedsize` for 151+ files), title stage uses `fileLoadToAddr` (resets `loadedsize=0` each call — immune), `mempResetPool(STAGE)` wipes allocations on CI transition but leaves `g_FileInfo[]` intact, CI manifest re-loads the same bodies/heads via `modeldefLoadToNew` → hits stale cache path → corruption. Fix: drop the `loadedsize==0` gate entirely; always recompute. Correctness for both EXTRAMEM and DEFAULT in one stroke. Cost: one extra `fileGetInflatedSize` (0x40-byte DMA + rzip header check) on cached loads — sub-ms at stage-transition time. Commits `e1205034` (Option A gated fix) → `b88df691` (gate removed, final). Merged `2e4f07d6`. Build clean 780/780 then 2/2 incremental. | `src/game/file.c:257` | Cold boot → main menu → CI Training (0x26). Expect no `MODELDEF: file N loaded torn` lines, no `FIX-B.2: modelnum N failed to load` warnings, no `ACCESS_VIOLATION PC=+0x1487fa`. Also test mission → endscreen → Next Mission (already-loaded files re-entered from fresh stage pool). |

| **B-160** | CRITICAL | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-04-16 (S304 — focused-roentgen)**. Menu pool slot leak → darkened/dead main menu on reopen + input authority desync. Root cause: `pdgui_menu_mainmenu.cpp`'s top-level close handler called `inputCtxPopDeferred(&g_CtxImGuiMenu)` + `menuPopDialog()` as two separate steps — if anything between open and close already popped the legacy stack (depth already 0), `menuPopDialog` early-returned on underflow WITHOUT releasing the pool slot acquired by `menuPushDialog`. Every subsequent `menuPushDialog(&g_CiMenuViaPcMenuDialog)` was then rejected by the pool's structural dedup (S299). Fix (three layers): (a) `menuPopDialog`'s underflow branch now calls `menupoolReleaseAll` if any slots remain, recovering the leak class; (b) new `menuPoolConsistencyCheck()` runs per-frame from `menuTick` — if the legacy `g_Menus[]` stack is empty but pool slots are active, they're released with WARNING + `menupoolDumpActive()` for diagnostics; (c) main menu renderer migrated to the S300 pool-owned ctx pattern — IsWindowAppearing now calls `menupoolAcquireDialog(menupoolDialogDef(dialog), &g_CtxImGuiMenu)` (attaches ctx to the live slot), close path drops the manual `inputCtxPopDeferred` and lets `menuPopDialog` → `menuCloseDialog` → `menupoolReleaseDialog` cascade pop the ctx atomically. | `src/game/menu.c`, `src/game/menutick.c`, `src/include/game/menu.h`, `port/fast3d/pdgui_menu_mainmenu.cpp` | Open main menu from CI free-roam; close with Esc or controller B; reopen 3-5 times. Expect: no `pool slot [main_menu] already active` warnings, clean `MENUPOOL: acquired main_menu gen=N` → `MENUPOOL: released main_menu gen=N` pairs across each cycle. Then stress the underflow path: rapid open/close during transition, or force-close while main menu open (match start, stage change) — watchdog WARNING `MENU: watchdog — legacy stack empty but N pool slot(s) active` should be absent on healthy flow, and if it ever fires, confirms auto-recovery rather than sticky leak. |

Cross-verify Issue #5 fixes compound with B-146 (pickups are now invisible to spawn validation via CDTYPE_ALL and also non-standable). |

| **B-151** | HIGH | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-04-16 (festive-saha — match-pipeline GAP-1)**. | **B-154** | HIGH | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-04-16 (silly-jepsen — textbox input leak to game actions)**. Playtest repro: open any menu with an `ImGui::InputText` focused (Nine-Slice Chrome "Mod Name", Audio Mod filters, Skin Editor name, connect-code field). Typing `E` there fires `ACTION_USE` (or whichever action the key is bound to) on the background player — letters leak into the game. Root cause: `pdguiProcessEvent` calls `actionmapDispatch(ev)` unconditionally on `SDL_KEYDOWN/KEYUP`. The `fireVk` gameplay-IMC gate (`gameplayInputSuppressed()`) only skips `g_ImcGameplay`/`g_ImcVehicle`; menu and debug IMCs still see the scancode, so mappings bound in those contexts still fire during text entry. Fix: gate `actionmapDispatch` on `ImGui::GetIO().WantCaptureKeyboard` for KEYDOWN/KEYUP. Esc and Enter (Return / KP_Enter) still pass through so InputText submission and dialog-cancel actions work unchanged. Event is also consumed (`return 1`) when ImGui eats the key, so nothing else downstream (legacy paths, context dispatch) sees it. | `port/fast3d/pdgui_backend.cpp` | Playtest: open Modding Hub → Nine-Slice Chrome, click in the Mod Name box, type "Esteemed" / "Weapon" — no ACTION_USE / ACTION_LOOK_UP / etc. fires on the background player. Esc still closes; Enter/Return submits. Re-verify with the Skin Editor name field and any MP connect-code entry.
| **B-155** | HIGH | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-04-16 (silly-jepsen — chrome mod absent from Mods list without restart)**. Nine-Slice Chrome "Save as Mod" writes `mods/UI Chrome/<slug>/mod.json` + tga, registers the chrome style via `pdguiThemeRegisterChromeModDir`, then reports "Saved & activated" — but the new mod didn't appear in Modding Hub → Mods until the next full `modmgrInit` at startup. Root cause: `modmgrScanDirectory()` is static and only runs during `modmgrInit`; no hot-rescan API existed. Fix: new public `modmgrRescanDirectory()` in `modmgr.c` — snapshots `{id, enabled, loaded}`, calls `modmgrScanDirectory()`, sorts, restores flags from the snapshot so pending in-memory toggles survive. Chrome save path now calls `modmgrRescanDirectory()` + `modmgrSetEnabled(idx, 1)` (for the new `user.<slug>.ui-chrome` id) + `modmgrSaveConfig()` + `pdguiModManagerRefreshSnapshot()` so the Mods tab shows the entry, enabled, pre-persisted to pd.ini. | `port/include/modmgr.h`, `port/src/modmgr.c`, `port/fast3d/pdgui_menu_moddinghub.cpp` | Playtest: Nine-Slice Chrome → load image → Save as Mod → open Modding Hub Mods tab — new mod appears checked/enabled immediately; quit + relaunch → mod is still enabled (config persisted). Also verify existing pending enable/disable toggles on other mods survive the rescan (should not be reverted).
| **B-156** | HIGH | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-04-16 (silly-jepsen — chrome mod missing from Video dropdown even after enable+save)**. After "Save as Mod", the new chrome texture never appeared in Settings → Video → UI Chrome Style alongside Procedural / Classic (base-game), even after toggling enabled + applying + restarting. Root cause: the chrome-manifest tokenizer `cjson_next` in `port/fast3d/pdgui_theme.cpp` consumed only digits for NUMBER tokens — no fractional part, no exponent. The mod.json the Nine-Slice tool writes includes a `chrome_authoring` block with `border_scale: 1.000` and `inset_pct` floats. When the top-level parser hit `chrome_authoring` it called `cjson_skip_value`, which entered the LBRACE branch; on the first `.` inside `1.000` the tokenizer returned `CJT_ERROR`, `cjson_skip_value` bailed early, and the outer parser resumed from the wrong position. `components` was never seen, `has_nineslice`/`out_tex_id` stayed empty, and `s_parseChromeManifest` returned 0 — so `pdguiThemeRegisterChromeModDir` + `s_scanModChromeStyles` both silently dropped the mod. The failure was restart-proof because the same parser runs at startup. Fix: extend `cjson_next`'s NUMBER branch to consume `.digits` and optional `[eE][+-]?digits` so `cjson_skip_value` can skip over floats inside unknown top-level objects. `cjson_int` still uses `strtol` (unchanged) — all chrome-manifest int reads are pure integers so this is safe. | `port/fast3d/pdgui_theme.cpp` | Playtest: Nine-Slice Chrome → Save as Mod → Settings → Video → UI Chrome Style dropdown now lists the new mod name between Procedural and any other discovered chrome style. Select it — the chrome applies live. Restart the game and confirm the selection persists and the style is still in the list.
| **B-152** | HIGH | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-04-16 (vigilant-robinson — stuck WASD after menu close)**. Playtest repro: back out of main menu, press+release W/A/S/D, character keeps walking that direction indefinitely. Re-opening and closing the menu clears it. Root cause: `actionmapPollFrame()` WASD→AXIS_MOVE synthesis block only writes `s_State[0][ACTION_AXIS_MOVE_X/Y].value` when at least one WASD key is held. When no controller is enumerated on player 0 (keyboard-only setup, or controller not plugged in for P0 slot), the controller-polling branch at line 803 is skipped, so nothing else resets `.value` each frame. After the user presses and releases W, the synthesis wrote `.value=1.0` during press; on release the block short-circuited (`mx=my=0`) and `.value` stayed at 1.0. The feedback loop: next frame reads stale `.value=1.0`, sets `analogStickActive=true`, skips synthesis — axis pegged forever. Menu open clears AXIS_MOVE via the `menuActive` branch at line 860, hence the "reopen fixes it" symptom. Fix: (a) track `p0CtrlDroveAxis` — true only when the controller actually wrote AXIS_MOVE this frame — and gate `analogStickActive` on it, so stale `.value` never suppresses the synthesis; (b) always assign the computed `mx/my` (including zero) when `!analogStickActive`, so WASD release writes `.value=0.0` and clears the stuck axis. | `port/src/actionmap.cpp` | Playtest: keyboard-only; open main menu → close → press+release W, A, S, D individually. Character should stop moving on each release. Verify the fix survives alt-tab, menu cycling, and mixed keyboard+controller input. |
| **B-153** | HIGH | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-04-16 (vigilant-robinson — double main menu on reopen)**. Playtest repro: close main menu, reopen with Esc/controller — the new instance renders overlaid (two copies) or with an unusually dark backdrop. Root cause: `menuPushDialog()` auto-opens every `dialogdef->nextsibling` at the same layer (menu.c:1553-1576). `g_CiMenuViaPauseMenuDialog`'s nextsibling is `g_CiOptionsViaPauseMenuDialog` — so pushing the main menu also loads the CI Options sibling. `menuRenderDialogs` renders the "other" sibling alongside curdialog whenever `type != 0 || transitionfrac >= 0`; both dialogdefs route through `pdguiHotswapCheck` and each queues its renderer (`renderMainMenu` for CiMenu, `renderCiSettingsRedirect` for CiOptions). CiSettings renderer calls `pdguiPopupDarkenBehind(0.55f)` — compounding with the main menu's own frame, producing "darker background / two copies overlaid". Normal operation has `transitionfrac=-1` → sibling skipped, but under rapid close+reopen the transition state can land in the visible window. Fix: new `menuDialogIsCurrent(dialog)` predicate in `menu.c` (scans `g_Menus[i].curdialog` across player slots) + guard at the top of `renderMainMenu` and `renderCiSettingsRedirect` — early-return when invoked for a sibling preload. Renderers consumed (return 1) so legacy fallback is not triggered. | `src/game/menu.c`, `src/include/game/menu.h`, `port/fast3d/pdgui_menu_mainmenu.cpp` | Playtest: open main menu → Esc to close → Esc to reopen immediately (< 1s). Single menu copy, normal backdrop. Also test swipe transitions to verify legitimate sibling renders during active swipe are still skipped by this guard (intentional — the ImGui hotswap world doesn't swipe; confirm no visual regression on CI Options swipe paths, should there be any). |
| **B-144** | MED | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-04-16 (S294)**. `matchConfigAddBot()` and `SVC_ROOM_SETTINGS` client read both called `matchConfigMaxBotsForHumans(1)` — hardcoded human count of 1 let the cap sit too high in 2-8 human matches. Fix: new `matchConfigCountHumans()` helper, both callsites switched. SVC_ROOM_SETTINGS client rebuild also switched to `matchConfigChooseBotTeam(2)` (matching host) and now zeros stale slot entries beyond the new bot count. | `port/src/net/matchsetup.c`, `port/src/net/netmsg.c` | 2-4 humans + bots room; add bots up to cap and confirm cap respects actual human count; team assignment matches host after host-initiated bot count change. |
| **B-141** | MED | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-04-17 (S323 — magical-mahavira, three-tier pacing)**. Audio skips / pauses, not reproducible on demand — `~200` underruns per 30s window per the S301 summary. Root cause identified: original N64 amgrFrame brake threshold of **1100 stereo samples = 50ms cushion** was smaller than typical PC main-thread hitches (50–100ms), so queue drained below the 128-sample SDL underrun threshold routinely. Producer/consumer math at 22050 Hz stereo × 60 Hz NTSC is 368 vs 367.5 stereo samples/frame = net +0.5/frame drift, so from a cold queue it took ~36 seconds to reach the 1100 target — meaning startup + hitch-recovery were both slow. **Fix**: three-tier production pacing in `src/lib/audiomgr.c::amgrFrame`. (tier 1) queue > **3000** → push 184 brake. (tier 2) queue 2500–3000 → push 368 steady (legacy drift rate). (tier 3) queue < 2500 → push **736** fast-fill (4 naudio chunks of 184; fits in the 3072-byte info->data allocation). Steady state now oscillates 2800–3000 (~128–136ms cushion, 3× original). Cold fill to 2500 takes 7 frames (~115ms) instead of 36 seconds. PAL retains its 552/frame rate (already sufficient for 50Hz). | `src/lib/audiomgr.c` | Playtest: play any arena for 60s, tail `pd-client.log` for `AUDIO[B-141]: 30s summary` lines. Expect `underruns=0` steady state; any residual underruns correlate with actual main-loop stalls > 135ms. `nullProducer` count should stay zero if `sndInit` ran. `buffered(samples) min` should now hover 2800–3000 instead of 900–1100. |
| **B-126** | HIGH | **MITIGATED S234 (FIX-A)**. Silent crash ~8 min in MP. Root cause: deep AI chains exhaust 8 MB stack → `-fstack-protector-strong` canary smash → SIGABRT → handler double-faults on corrupt stack → silent death. A.1 stack depth cap, A.2 generation tokens, A.3 SIGABRT handler hardened, A.4 per-chr stack watermark. | `chr.c`, `chraction.c`, `crash.c`, `main.c` | 31-bot repro test to confirm crash eliminated. |
| **B-112** | HIGH | **MITIGATED S234 (FIX-A). ROOT CAUSE CONFIRMED S357.** Chr rbx corruption in `chraTick` during 31-bot matches. Two classes: (1) stack depth — single-bot `chraTick→chraiExecute→action dispatch→collision/combat` chain can exceed available stack in extreme 31-bot combat scenarios; `chraiExecute` is iterative (not recursive), AI commands never call `chraTick`, all array bounds correct. FIX-A.1 (512 KB guard) skips bots when stack is low. (2) stale pointer — bot respawn never frees/reallocates chr slots (`botSpawn` teleports in place), so class 2 is rare; FIX-A.2 generation tokens detect the edge case. B-12 Phase 3 cleared: participant pool sized to `MAX_MPCHRS=40`, chr slots sized to `PLAYERCOUNT()+numchrs+10=42`, `AVOID_UB=1` guards the known N64 `mpCalculateAwards` `playerrankings[1]` overflow. No new fix needed. | `chraction.c`, `chr.c`, `types.h`, `crash.c` | 31-bot repro test to confirm FIX-A eliminates crashes in practice. |

Defensively correct regardless of whether it is the sole root cause. | `src/game/mplayer/mplayer.c:3734` | Fresh 32-bot Chicago match, idle 30 s, open pause → kill counter should be 0/0. |

### Open without B-number (2026-04-13 Chicago playtest, carried forward)

These are in `tasks-current.md` punch list and `context/scratch/archive/2026-04-13/README.md` — promotion to formal B-numbers deferred until the 2026-04-13 drop is playtest-verified (several may have already cleared).

- **Bug C (post-game endscreen partial render)** — scrim + title-bar rectangle render but content body is invisible. Six hypotheses in `scratch/archive/2026-04-13/session-state-endgame-crash.md` §4c. **Instrumented S295 (festive-saha)** + **ENHANCED S301 (confident-chaum)**: `renderMpEndscreen` now emits comprehensive `ENDSCREEN.DIAG:` lines tagged for grep — fresh-entry with `g_MpPlayerNum / g_NetMode / challengeResult`, full geometry dump (`sf / menu / pad / win / contentAvail / scroll`), body-child geometry (`contentW / contentH / innerCRA`), rankings-count (`rankings built count=N teamsMode=…`), awards-path (SHOW|SKIP with award strings + medals bitfield), and actions-section checkpoint. Capped at 80 prints per match via `ENDSCREEN_DIAG_MAX_PRINTS` so oscillation can't flood the log. Existing `ENDSCREEN:` LOG_WARNINGs on `Begin=false` and `contentH clamped` retained. Next repro should produce enough log evidence on the first frame to pick which of the six hypotheses fired.
- **Bug D (invisible networked bots on Chicago)** — bots visible on minimap and audible but not rendered in world. Likely chr generation token mismatch (FIX-A.2 area); may have cleared with recent drops. **Instrumented S301 (confident-chaum)**: `CHR.DIAG:` prefix on three points — `botmgrAllocateBot` logs chrnum / body / head / body_id / head_id at entry and the returned model ptr (with WARNING if NULL), `bodyAllocateModel` logs body_canon / head_canon lookups (WARNING if catalog ID is NULL for a non-negative bodynum/headnum), `botSpawn` before (`BOT.SPAWN` breadcrumb + `CHR.DIAG: botSpawn …`) and after (`CHR.DIAG: botSpawn DONE …`) with the full invisible-state check: `WARNING chrnum=… is INVISIBLE after botSpawn (model=NULL|HIDDEN|VOID(-1))`. An invisible bot now leaves an explicit fingerprint identifying which field was the cause.
- **Chicago silent crash ~9 s** — needs VEH log + symbolify. **Instrumented S301 (confident-chaum)**: new `port/src/crashbreadcrumb.c` ring buffer (256 slots × 112 bytes, static BSS) dumped by VEH / UEF / SIGABRT / Linux `sigaction` paths directly into `pd.crash.log` / `pd.log` / `pd-client.log` under a `CRASH.DIAG: breadcrumb ring dump follows:` heading. Push sites: mainTick heartbeat per frame, lvTick per frame, chraTickBg per frame, chraTick per chr (slot + action + race), bwalkTick per player (pos + room + floorroom), botSpawn, botmgrAllocateBot, mainChangeToStage, SVC_STAGE_START send/receive. Ring holds ~130 ms of CHR.TICK pushes (32 bots × 60 Hz = 1920/s), enough to identify which chr + action was being ticked at the moment of crash. No dependency on ordinary log output — even a crash that corrupts sysLogPrintf will still dump the ring.
- **Airbase Start-Match 0xc0000005 / no-response** — log shows manifest OK but no SVC_STAGE_START. May share root cause with Bug A (fix shipped `d37e9677`). **Instrumented S301 (confident-chaum)**: `MATCHSTART.DIAG:` tag on every decision point in the match-start chain — `matchStart` entry / pre-mpStartMatch / post-mpStartMatch, `netServerStageStart` entry + send outcome (`SVC_STAGE_START sent to room 0x… (combat sim)` or `…broadcast to all clients`), `netmsgSvcStageStartWrite` begin, `netmsgSvcStageStartRead` begin. Each bail has an explicit reason message (`BAILED reason=empty_stage_id`, `SVC_STAGE reject — client state=… not in LOBBY/GAME/PREPARING`). With both sides tagged, one Airbase repro is enough to pin the decision point that stopped progress.

| B-334 | Skedar GPU benchmark behavior did not match CPU benchmark behavior, and benchmark Skedar jump / wall-walk paths were not functioning. Fix: GPU swarm defaults to GPU_FULL with GPU_POS_ONLY retained as diagnostic, GPU readback carries jump/surface intent bits, CPU/GPU swarm share `swarmTestApplyMovementIntent`, surface contact correction applies through `chrSurfaceLocoApplyContactPos` instead of world-ground `chrSetPos`, and `chrStartSkJump` null-checks targets before distance checks. | 2026-05-18 -- `skswarm` queued build, static guard, `skedar_swarm_cpu_behavior_smoke`, `skedar_swarm_gpu_behavior_smoke` |
| B-201 | Device Training (DT) and Holo Training (HT) drill-in broken by the same pool-dedup collision as B-200. `g_DtListMenuDialog` + `g_DtDetailsMenuDialog` (and `Ht*` counterparts) all shared `MENU_TYPE_TRAINING`; Details is pushed on top of List via `menuPushDialog` so the second push was rejected silently and the Details screen never appeared. Fix: split into `MENU_TYPE_DT_LIST` / `MENU_TYPE_DT_DETAILS` / `MENU_TYPE_DT_RESULT` and `MENU_TYPE_HT_LIST` / `MENU_TYPE_HT_DETAILS` / `MENU_TYPE_HT_RESULT`, mirroring the FR split. Completed + Failed share RESULT (mutually exclusive per flow). Bio / Hangar stay on `MENU_TYPE_TRAINING` (no stacked Details dialog at this time). | 2026-04-19 -- menupool.h, menupool.c |
| B-200 | Firing Range menu accepted weapon click but silently did nothing (CI firing range non-functional). Root cause: `g_FrWeaponListMenuDialog`, `g_FrTrainingInfoPreGameMenuDialog`, `g_FrCompletedMenuDialog`, `g_FrFailedMenuDialog` all registered as `MENU_TYPE_TRAINING`. The FR flow legitimately stacks Weapon List -> Difficulty -> Pre-Game Info, so the second push was rejected by pool dedup (`pool slot [training] already active`). Fix: split FR sub-dialogs into dedicated pool types (`MENU_TYPE_FR_WEAPON_LIST`, `MENU_TYPE_FR_DIFFICULTY`, `MENU_TYPE_FR_INFO`, `MENU_TYPE_FR_RESULT`) following the MP_SETTINGS precedent. Registered `g_FrDifficultyMenuDialog` (was missing). Addressed the S300 concern at tasks-current.md:1547. **Follow-up (DT/HT)**: fixed in B-201. | 2026-04-19 -- 57c33870 -- menupool.h, menupool.c |

Five unsafe sites found and fixed: (1) `bondbike.c:bbikeHandleActivate` — early return if `objFindBboxRodata` returns NULL (can't compute dismount distances); (2) `chr.c:3050` — struct copy `thing->bbox = *bbox` guarded with zero-init fallback; (3) `propobj.c:glassDestroy` — `shardsCreate` now skipped if bbox NULL (object removed without shards); (4) `propobj.c:doorIsObjInRange` — returns `doorIsPosInRange(..., 0, isbike)` (point check) if bbox NULL; (5) `propobj.c:func0f069850` — `objCalculateGeoBlockFromBboxAndMtx` now skipped with empty block fallback if bbox NULL and rodata19 NULL. All other callsites confirmed safe (NULL-guarded or passing through S308-patched `objGetLocal*`/`objGetRotatedLocal*` helpers). | 2026-04-17 — S318 — bondbike.c, chr.c, propobj.c |

Fix: `netReadyGateOnClientLeft()` + `netReadyGateAbortForRoom()` called from `roomLeave()` in `room.c`; defensive "room missing" guard at top of `readyGateTickCountdown()`. No protocol bump. | **B-179** | HIGH | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-04-19 (sweet-chandrasekhar-7d64da — S371)**. 22/32 bots defaulting to mphead=0 (Joanna Dark head) in MP. Six specific heads load with parts=0 (torn modeldefs): `head_dark_snow`, `head_ddshock`, `head_carrington`, `head_ddsniper`, `head_president`, `head_cassandra`. Root cause: `validateModeldef` in `port/src/modelcatalog.c` was the remaining `numparts <= 0 ⟹ INVALID` guard that B-166 (`modeldefLoad`) and B-167 (`propobj.c` bbox walkers) did not propagate to. When a head's modeldef had parts=0 but valid rootnode/skel (the six heads above), the catalog marked it INVALID, and every `catalogValidateHeadId()` call (SVC_STAGE_START read paths in `netmsg.c:1366/1421/4792`) fell back through `catalogGetSafeHead → CATALOG_FALLBACK_HEAD` to head 0 (dark_combat = Joanna Dark). 22 of 32 bots either picked one of those six heads directly or had their default paired head routed through this fallback. Fix: `validateModeldef` now only rejects `numparts <= 0` when `category == MODELCAT_BODY` (bodies need skeletal parts for `body0f02ce8c` merge; falling back to dark_combat is the correct UX for torn bodies). Heads pass through with parts=0 — rendering walks rootnode DL nodes, `modelAttachHead` only needs rootnode, `modelGetPart` already returns NULL on parts=0 and every caller tolerates that (sunglasses/hudpiece toggles simply skipped). Build clean 774/774. `PerfectDark.exe` 53,202,559 / `PerfectDarkServer.exe` 23,141,856. | `port/src/modelcatalog.c` | Playtest: MP Combat Sim with 30+ bots — bots should wear their actual assigned heads (Cassandra, President, Carrington, DD-variants, Dark Snow) rather than 22 identical Joanna Dark heads. `CATALOG: [idx] file 0x%04x — numparts=0 out of range (INVALID)` warnings at validation time should disappear for the six head filenums. |
| **B-180** | HIGH | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-04-19 (cascade of B-179 — S371)**. Missing necks on all character models. Cascades from B-179: when 22/32 bots were being downgraded to the Joanna Dark head, the head-to-body fit was wrong and neck geometry (part of each head's own rootnode mesh) appeared missing or mismatched. With B-179 fix installed, each bot now loads its actual head (including neck geometry) and attaches via `modelAttachHead` to the body's headspot. `bodyCalculateHeadOffset` still runs for cross-gender/cross-type pairings to realign the head vertically. | `port/src/modelcatalog.c` (cascade fix) | Playtest: after B-179 playtest passes, verify each bot has a visible neck connecting head to body — no floating heads, no disconnected meshes. |
| **B-181** | MED | **FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-04-19 (interesting-nobel-184c6c)**. Wrong weapons spawning in match — map has 10 weapon markers vs target 16, fallback path was injecting unexpected weapons outside the selected weapon set. Root cause: `setupCreateProps` fallback (`src/game/setup.c:2467`, added in commit `0b62fc68` to force spawn-with-weapon when world pickups < desiredPickups) used `catalogIdByRuntime(ASSET_WEAPON, g_MpSetup.weapons[i])` to look up a catalog ID from an MPWEAPON_* index — but the runtime cache for ASSET_WEAPON is indexed by catalog *array position* (`e->runtime_index = i` in `assetcatalog_base_extended.c`, not by weapon_id), so MPWEAPON_FALCON2 (0x01) resolved to `"base:falcon2_silencer"` (MPWEAPON_FALCON2_SILENCER at array position 1). Additional issue: the fallback set `spawn_weapon_id` but never re-derived `spawnWeaponNum` (matchStart already ran), so the current match continued to use the pre-fallback spawn weapon, and the wrong ID persisted into the NEXT match when matchStart re-read it. Fix: replaced the `catalogIdByRuntime` lookup with a catalog scan that matches `ext.weapon.weapon_id == mpw` (same pattern as `savefile.c:816` and `buildSpawnWeaponList`). Skip MPWEAPON_NONE/SHIELD/DISABLED. Always override (not just if empty) so any stale out-of-set spawn weapon is corrected. Re-derive `spawnWeaponNum` immediately so the current match's player/bot spawn respects the fallback choice. Build clean 774/774. `PerfectDark.exe` 53,195,903 / `PerfectDarkServer.exe` 23,140,832. | `src/game/setup.c` | Playtest: Car Park solo sim with a small weapon set (e.g. Pistols) + enough bots to trigger the fallback — only weapons from the configured weapon set should appear, both on pads (already correct) and on player/bot spawn. Check `SETUP: world pickups ... forcing spawn-with-weapon fallback id='...'` log line names a catalog ID that is in the configured set. |

S589 closed a concrete null/attach-point crash shape in `body0f02ce8c()`: positive-head catalog loads now use `catalogGetHeadModeldefChecked`, `modelAllocateRwData(headmodeldef)` only runs under `headmodeldef != NULL`, the pre-load `g_HeadsAndBodies[headnum]` read is bounds-gated, and `modelmgrAttachHead()` requires a non-NULL headspot node. Static coverage pins both guards. | `src/game/body.c`; `tests/test_catalog_checked.cpp` | Build is pending because wrapper-only `hguard589` stalled in `Generate Headers [pd_headers]` with empty stdout/stderr and no `pd-tests.exe`. S589 prevents one missing-head/missing-headspot crash path in character assembly, but geometry corruption itself remains an open visual-content fingerprint until a playtest/log captures the exact body/head/modeldef/renderer path. | `src/game/body.c`, `src/lib/model.c`, `port/fast3d/` | Playtest after B-179/S589 — check character model geometry integrity. | **B-184** | MED | **ROOT CAUSE IDENTIFIED + FIXED-PENDING-PLAYTEST 2026-04-19 (elated-hugle-7ec221 — S384)**. Commit `fe107e3e` (2026-04-17, M4 optimisation) collapsed `ALIGN16(val)` to `(val)` — was valid for SIZE-only uses but broke POINTER uses at `src/game/gfxmemory.c:154` (`g_GfxMemPos = (u8 *)ALIGN16((uintptr_t)g_GfxMemPos)`). `Vtx` is 12 bytes, so `gfxAllocateVertices(count)` advances `g_GfxMemPos` by `count*12`; for odd counts the no-op macro leaves `g_GfxMemPos mod 16 != 0`. The next `gfxAllocateColours()` then uses `g_GfxMemPos` as the base for the `Col` array the subsequent `G_COL` command binds as `vcn`, so `gfx_sp_set_vertex_colors` reads misaligned bytes from an offset-by-12-mod-16 source — each draw call gets a consistent-but-wrong vertex-colour array, producing the per-object tints Mike screenshotted (yellow computer props, cyan elevator top, olive character faces, dark-red `env={40 0a 0a ff}` dominant on ~14% of draws in the S382.3 diag). Also explains the S382 diag "`vtx0` = `ff ff f0 f0` with `lighting=0`" rows starting at G_COL #101 — same misread pattern from a drifted pointer. Fix is the same one-line change as B-193 — `src/include/constants.h:79` restored to `((((val) + 0xf) | 0xf) ^ 0xf)`. The existing S382 `GFX.DIAG: G_COL` instrumentation in `port/fast3d/gfx_pc.cpp::gfx_sp_set_vertex_colors` is retained; post-fix log should show `vtx0` stabilising to authored colour bytes (typically `ff ff ff ff` or grayscale per-prop) rather than drift-dependent bytes. Build clean 777/777. `PerfectDark.exe` 53,232,611 / `PerfectDarkServer.exe` 23,154,674. **[Prior-pass history]:** Four prior passes (S372 SAVED-EFFECT strip; S381 effect_normal_tint mod folder rule-out; S382 visual-evidence + G_COL diag ship; S382 fourth-pass static diff audit of the AP Phase sprint). All rule-outs stand (SAVED EFFECT dead, effect_normal_tint inert, meshDebug inert, dlights uniform-white, no G_LIGHTING clears on char/prop draws, AP Phase 3 rename byte-identical, S322/S323 IS4MB strips touched zero render state). The tint is NOT normal-visualisation, NOT UV corruption, NOT texture selection — it was vertex-colour data misread from a misaligned vcn pointer, exactly matching the "per-object consistent tint on correct textures" pattern. | `src/include/constants.h` | Playtest CI main menu + free-roam: character skin + clothing should be their authored colours (not olive-green); computer props should be their authored tint (not yellow/cyan); elevator top should be gray (not cyan); doors should be their authored colour (not yellow). `GFX.DIAG: G_COL` lines in `pd-client.log` should show stable `vtx0` bytes that match the visible prop colour. Repeat on other stages (Dam, Carrington Villa, Felicity) to regression-check. |
